#2110. Arsenal injury news: Mikel Arteta drops update on whether David Raya will be fit to face Man City

By Lewis Blain | 25 Nov 2026 07:29

Arsenal boss Mikel Arteta has refused to confirm whether David Raya will be available for their Premier League showdown with Manchester City on Sunday afternoon.

The Spaniard missed Tuesday night's 1-1 draw with Borussia Dortmund in the Champions League after picking up a shoulder injury against Newcastle United at the weekend.

Kepa Arrizabalaga deputised at the Emirates Stadium, with Arteta offering his backing to the goalkeeper should Raya remain unavailable this weekend.

Raya left the pitch in significant pain during Arsenal's 2-2 draw with the Magpies on Saturday night, prompting fears that he could face a lengthy spell on the sidelines.

However, subsequent scans proved positive, with the Spanish goalkeeper avoiding a serious injury and not requiring surgery.

There have been suggestions that Raya could only be sidelined for a matter of days and might therefore be in contention to face City, but Arteta was unwilling to offer any guarantees after the Dortmund draw.

"No, we have nothing to add today unfortunately," the Arsenal manager said (via the Evening Standard). "We need to know first how bad it is and whether he has a chance or not [to face Man City]."

Arsenal will therefore have to wait for further clarity on their first-choice goalkeeper, with Kepa potentially required to retain his place against Enzo Maresca's side, who also drew at home to Napoli this midweek.

Kepa produced a reasonably solid display in Germany, with Arteta describing his performance as "really good" and insisting that Arsenal fully trust their goalkeepers.

The Spaniard made three saves and received a 7.5 Sofascore rating, making him the Gunners' third-best performer on the night. His expected goals prevented figure of 0.38 also reflected a positive contribution.

There were, however, a few moments that offered cause for concern. Kepa was wrong-footed for Dortmund's goal, while he parried a disallowed effort back into a dangerous area and did not look as assured as Raya when dealing with crosses.

Arteta is nevertheless confident in his deputy, saying: "We fully trust our keepers and if he needs to play against City, he will play."

It is encouraging that Kepa has Arteta's backing, but there would understandably be some apprehension about him starting against a side of City's calibre. Raya's command of his penalty area and reliability have made him an important figure for Arsenal, and his absence would be a significant setback.

However, if the first-choice goalkeeper is not ready, Kepa will have to step up when called upon.

#2108. AC Milan 5-1 Frosinone: Counter-attacking clinic sees Rossoneri run riot

AC Milan put on a show for their fans at San Siro on Sunday evening as they beat Frosinone 5-1 to move back into the top four.

It was a game that promised entertainment from the early stages, and Milan took command thanks to an own-goal from Gabriele Calvani and a much-needed goal from Goncalo Ramos, before Patrizio Masini scored to halve the deficit.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

The Rossoneri ran away with things in the second half, with Ardon Jashari and Alphadjo Cisse netting off the bench before Christian Pulisic got a goal of his own to add some gloss to the scoreline. There were chances to make it an even bigger margin, but five would have to do.

Given that Lazio, Roma, Como and Juventus had all won prior to kick-off, it was important that Milan matched their results to keep up the pressure at the top end of the table, and they did so in emphatic fashion.

Ruben Amorim made just one change to the starting line-up from the side that won against Genoa before the break, with Alphadjo Cisse dropping out and Davide Bartesaghi coming in. It was uncertain from the line-up named what the system would be, as has often been the case with the Portuguese coach so far.

Frosinone started the game better and called Mike Maignan into action inside the first minute with a close-range header that was saved, though the flag was up. Then, a deflected strike from Masini on the edge of the box ended up scraping the upright and going behind for a corner.

However, it was Milan who struck first at San Siro. Luka Modric deserves the plaudits for an outstanding through ball to find Adrien Rabiot’s run in behind, and the Frenchman’s square pass was heading towards Christian Pulisic’s run but was turned into his own net by Calvani, leaving Palmisani rooted.

The frantic pace of the early stages continued, as a lovely exchange between Diego Moreira and Pulisic saw a threatening cross turned behind. A few minutes later, Yunus Musah found Moreira who had a shot deflected for a corner, which Goncalo Ramos rose and met well but headed over the bar.

Frosinone kept providing reminders that they too pose a threat going forward, such as on the quarter-hour mark when Kvernadze surged down the left and got off a low shot from a narrow angle that Maignan had to parry behind. The corner wasn’t properly dealt with, and Cittadini flashed one across the six-yard box that Rabiot had to put into the stands.

Just like with the first goal, Milan punished Frosinone for not making the most of their spell of pressure. Goncalo Ramos was the man who started and finished a flowing counter-attack, robbing Calvani of possession before running towards goal. Musah’s through ball was run onto by the electric Moreira, who found Rabiot, and the Frenchman’s cut-back was tucked away by the Portuguese.

The two-goal lead was very quickly halved though, as Frosinone got the goal their pressure had threatened. Kvernadze’s run down the left and cut-back from the byline was scuffed onwards by Ghedjemis into the path of Masini, who toe-poked it in at the far post.

In the 34th minute, there was a moment that enraged the Milan players, staff and fans. The referee blew his whistle for a shirt pull on Rabiot just as Milan went through on goal with two men in behind and just the goalkeeper to beat. The official apologised, but it felt like a certain goal had been taken away.

It was a half played at a hundred miles an hour, and it ended that way too. Pulisic flashed an effort over the bar from a good position, while Davide Bartesaghi’s low cross towards Ramos was cut out in the nick of time as Milan continued to get joy down the flanks.

Amorim opted to make an intriguing change at half-time, bringing off Bartesaghi and replacing him with Ardon Jashari. This change in turn saw Musah shift to left-back, a role that he played against Genoa in the last match, and from minute one.

The change proved to be an absolute masterstroke, as Jashari scored inside three minutes. A ball was sent long to Ramos who knocked it down to Rabiot, whose first touch was a lovely through ball to the Swiss midfield. It looked like he had overrun it with the square option not used, but he lashed a shot in at the near post.

Not long after the goal, Ramos gestured towards the bench that he felt some discomfort. He wanted to play through it but Amorim took no risks, opting to bring Alphadjo Cisse on to lead the line.

There was a rather rare occurrence in the 54th minute, as Milan were awarded an indirect free-kick after Palmisani picked up a back pass from Calvani. Modric laid it off to Rabiot, but the goalkeeper made up for his error with a good save.

Continuing the trend of impact substitutions, Cisse got his ninth goal of the season right on the hour mark. A Frosinone corner was cleared and Pulisic found Moreira who had the Italian for company, and he finished the through ball by the Belgian past Palmisani with his trademark composure. The offside flag went up, but after a long check the goal was given.

Milan continued to look like scoring every time they got out of their own half, and Pulisic joined the fun in the 68th minute with a goal of his own. Frosinone again left one man back for a corner that was cleared, allowing the American to easily outpace Terzic with a run from inside his own half, dinking over Palmisani to make it 5-1.

With 20 minutes left on the clock, Amorim brought Strahinja Pavlovic and Pervis Estupinan on for Diawara and Musah. The final change saw Omari Hutchinson replace Pulisic.

Pavlovic looked far from 100% when he came on, appearing dizzy at certain moments. The head coach seemed to suggest coming off so as not to risk things, but he played on.

Milan had chances to make the scoreline even more impressive, with Moreira and Hutchinson squandering a couple of chances on the break. However, at full-time the team were rightly applauded by the San Siro crowd.

#2106. PSG vs Roma: Luis Enrique issues warning before Roma clash

By Axel Clody | 25 Nov 2026 07:34

Paris Saint-Germain are still coming to terms with the loss of Desire Doue for at least six months because of a serious knee injury, and are making their final preparations to face Roma in the Champions League. One of Luis Enrique's biggest concerns ahead of the game is the red-hot form of Donyell Malen, Roma's top scorer.

At a press conference on Tuesday (24 November), the Spanish manager went out of his way to praise the Dutch forward, who already has 16 goals and four assists in 15 games for Roma this season. Luis Enrique highlighted Malen's improvement since January, when he moved to Italy and developed enormously under Gian Piero Gasperini, forcing Paris Saint-Germain to draw up a special containment plan.

'I am impressed with Donyell Malen and the number of goals he has scored, he has been great this season. Since he started playing as a centre-forward, he has been doing very well. We have to be very careful' - the manager said.

Paris Saint-Germain face Roma on Wednesday (25 November) at 8pm UK time at the Parc des Princes, on matchday five of the league phase. The Spanish boss will try to extend his side's perfect record to stay top of the table, while the Italian manager knows the importance of a surprise result to move towards the knockout phase, as Roma sit 20th with four points.

More than his goalscoring record, the number 14 concerns Luis Enrique because his profile enhances Gasperini's style of play: attacking runs in behind, control of the penalty area and a great ability to finish. Before Malen, Roma suffered from the lack of a player with those characteristics, as neither Artem Dovbyk nor Evan Ferguson had success in the first half of 2025-26.

The solution found by the manager was to use the winter transfer window to take the 27-year-old from Aston Villa, where he had few opportunities as a starter. In England, the Dutchman was used mostly as a winger by Unai Emery, but the Roma boss saw his potential to play in a more central role.

The six-month loan was the turning point for Roma, who climbed from sixth to third in Serie A, securing a return to the Champions League after an eight-year absence. With 14 goals and two assists in 18 league games, Malen was signed permanently from Aston Villa for £23m (€27m).

In 2026-27, thanks to the number 14's fluid partnership with Matias Soule, Paulo Dybala, Wesley, Rodrigo Mora and company, Roma are established in the top four and are Serie A title contenders, sitting second with 30 points, two behind leaders Lazio after 12 matches. Roma have not been Italian champions since 2000-01.

61 shots (30 on target) 26.5 per cent goal conversion rate An average of one goal every 70 minutes An average of 11 successful dribbles per game (52.5 per cent) 39 duels won (41.5 per cent) 10 fouls won Data from Sofascore

Mobility is one of the Dutch forward's trademarks, as he often drops close to team-mates to offer a passing option during build-up and to create space in the opposition defence for others to advance. Out of possession, Malen is also a key part of Roma's pressing to win the ball back in favourable conditions for a shot.

In the best year of his career, the Roma number 14 is the biggest threat to Paris Saint-Germain's defensive system, which knows it cannot afford to slip. Otherwise, Malen is likely to punish the French side with his technical ability and finishing efficiency, even when chances are rare.

#2105. Fifa, hacker rivendicano oltre 12 GB di dati sottratti

Il gruppo HBHC sostiene di aver tentato senza successo di estorcere denaro all’organizzazione calcistica.

La Fifa ha confermato la sottrazione di dati interni durante un recente attacco informatico. Secondo quanto riportato da SportMediaset, Il gruppo di criminalità informatica Handsome Boy Hacking Club sostiene di aver prelevato oltre 12 GB di dati e di aver tentato, senza successo, di estorcere denaro all’organizzazione.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

La conferma è riportata dal sito specializzato in cybersecurity The Record, citato da SportMediaset. L’organizzazione calcistica, che ha sede in Svizzera, ha dichiarato che gli hacker hanno violato i suoi sistemi, ottenendo un «accesso non autorizzato a un insieme limitato di informazioni personali e aziendali».

L’organizzazione non ha risposto a ulteriori domande sulla natura dell’incidente. La scorsa settimana, il gruppo Handsome Boy Hacking Club (HBHC) ha dichiarato di aver sottratto oltre 12 GB di dati alla Fifa. Il gruppo ha anche riferito di aver tentato senza successo di estorcerle denaro.

Tra le informazioni sottratte figurerebbero documenti d’identità e relativi all’immigrazione, documentazione finanziaria e legale, informazioni sulla governance e altri dati.

#2104. Player Ratings: AC Milan 5-1 Frosinone – Rabiot unstoppable; super attack

AC Milan clinched a comfortable win against Frosinone and individual brilliance certainly had a hand in that. Below are the player ratings for the Rossoneri.

Ahead of what will be a busy month, Milan knew that a win tonight would do them a lot of good, so that’s exactly what they managed to achieve. Goals from Goncalo Ramos, Ardon Jashari, Alphadjo Cisse and Christian Pulisic set them up nicely, as well as an early own-goal.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

If you haven’t already, make sure to check out how we decide the player ratings. From the scale to what affects the rating, we have compiled everything there.

🥇 Rabiot (8 – MOTM): He just can’t help himself from getting goal contributions! Two more assists today, three if you count the own-goal, and his running is becoming a proper weapon for Milan. He’s just unstoppable when he gets going.

👔 Amorim (7.5): I think this was more or less a home run tactically. Frosinone were always going to try to have a high line, so the head coach exploited that with the help of Rabiot and the passing abilities of his teammates. Benching Cisse was bold, but it turned out to be just the right move.

Maignan (6): I’m not sure he could have done better on the goal, as it was really hard to predict where the cross would end up. Anyway, he made a couple of decent stops and was good with his feet, even if there was a scare in the first half.

Gila (6.5): Let me just say straight off the bat that he wasn’t far away from a 7. However, I think he could have done better to stop the cross on the goal conceded, and he had a few really poor passes as well. On the plus side, he was decent in possession overall and had a key clearance in the second half.

De Winter (6.5): He continues to marshal the defence well, though he lacked concentration on a few occasions today. It didn’t prove to be costly and similarly to Gila, there was definitely more good than harm, but the rating is affected a tad.

Diawara (6.5): I don’t think he could have got to the ball quicker on the goal, not to mention that it’s hard to anticipate such a deflection. As far as I’m concerned, it was solid defending from him, including some nice clearances to stop a few dangerous crosses.

Bartesaghi (6): Defensively he did okay, which was all that was required of him, but the main contributions were in attack. I think we saw some really good runs forward from him, although the crosses had mixed results. It seems fair with a 6 for him.

Moreira (7.5): His run before the second goal was crucial, of course, and he also managed to get an assist (good pass in the end, as VAR confirmed). For a higher rating, I would have liked to see more sharpness in the final third, as he had a couple of very good shooting opportunities that he missed.

Modric (8): My word, the pass before the first goal was just magnificent! That alone shows just how much he means to this team, but he also continued to do well with his passing and movement after that. For me, the rating does deserve to be that high, but maybe I’m blinded by the aforementioned pass.

Musah (7): He did really well to unleash Moreira just before the second goal, playing it perfectly. On the goal conceded, it was just unlucky in my opinion, he did everything right by getting a toe on it. The block at the start of the second half was just brilliant too, showcasing good defensive skills.

Pulisic (7.5): Amorim always says he lets the pitch do the talking and that was the case today. He wasn’t flashy by any means, but did very good work in silence (such as the movement before the own-goal) and was eventually rewarded with the goal. Stunning finish by the way. An 8 perhaps?

Rabiot (8 – MOTM): He just can’t help himself from getting goal contributions! Two more assists today, three if you count the own-goal, and his running is becoming a proper weapon for Milan. He’s just unstoppable when he gets going.

Ramos (7.5): Finally, back on target! Overall, it was a very encouraging one for him as the goal was the result of great positioning and hard work, which helped on the other goals too (opening up space). He could have had a second too if he hadn’t been stopped by the referee.

Jashari (7): That’s one way to make an impact! The finish was fantastic and really surprised the goalkeeper. He had a few more good runs after that, so this was definitely a performance that he can build on.

Cisse (7): It was an easy finish on the goal, it has to be said, but he still timed the run very well. He also showed off some good understanding in the centre-forward, which was nice to see. It wasn’t nice to see the unnecessary yellow card at the end, though.

Pavlovic (N/A): I didn’t see enough to give him a rating.

Estupinan (N/A): I didn’t see enough to give him a rating.

Hutchinson (N/A): I didn’t see enough to give him a rating.

Amorim (7.5): I think this was more or less a home run tactically. Frosinone were always going to try to have a high line, so the head coach exploited that with the help of Rabiot and the passing abilities of his teammates. Benching Cisse was bold, but it turned out to be just the right move.

#2103. 'The main problem' - Enzo Maresca defends surprise Man City tactical decision after 2-2 Napoli draw

By Lewis Blain | 25 Nov 2026 07:48

Manchester City boss Enzo Maresca has explained some of his unexpected selection and tactical decisions after his side were held to a 2-2 draw by Napoli at the Etihad Stadium on Tuesday evening.

The result saw City twice come from behind, but their defensive problems continued as they dropped further points in the Champions League.

Maresca also acknowledged that his side are still searching for the right balance between attacking and defending following his arrival as Pep Guardiola's successor.

Maresca left Jeremy Doku and Iliman Ndiaye on the bench for the opening hour, while Phil Foden was also rested, prompting questions about his selection.

The City boss explained that his decisions were based on how he expected Napoli to set up, with the visitors having recently used a 3-5-2 or 3-5-1-1 formation.

"We expected Napoli to play 3-5-2 or 3-5-1-1 as they did in recent matches, so that would basically leave eight players in the middle, and hope to create more spaces down the wings," Maresca told Sky Sport Italia.

He wanted Rayan Cherki to operate out wide and exploit those spaces, although City struggled to turn their possession into enough chances during the first half.

Maresca also defended his decision to use Ayyoub Bouaddi in a deeper midfield role alongside Elliott Anderson, explaining that he wanted to maintain balance while allowing six players to attack.

However, the manager admitted that City's defensive shortcomings remain a significant concern.

"The main problem is that we are conceding too many goals, and we need to do something about that," he added.

Maresca also acknowledged that finding the right approach would take time, comparing City's transition to the challenges Arsenal and Manchester United experienced following the departures of Arsene Wenger and Sir Alex Ferguson.

It is not unusual for Maresca to rotate his City side during midweek fixtures, but leaving Doku, Ndiaye and Foden out of the starting lineup for a potentially important Champions League match was still a notable decision.

City ultimately rescued a point, but the draw could prove costly in their hopes of progressing in the competition, particularly given their recent struggles for consistency.

Perhaps Maresca also had this weekend's showdown with Premier League title rivals Arsenal in mind, with City travelling to the Emirates for Sunday's late kick-off.

That is not to suggest the manager deliberately sacrificed the Napoli fixture, but resting Foden and keeping two dangerous wide players in reserve could indicate that he was considering the demands of a huge domestic encounter.

City need to find greater defensive stability regardless of the opposition, as Maresca himself acknowledged, but Sunday's meeting with the Gunners may have been a factor in how he approached his midweek selection.

#2102. Cisse è il primo giocatore del Milan in doppia cifra. Sicuri che serva un altro attaccante?

Dieci gol stagionali, a novembre e alla prima vera stagione in Serie A. Con il colpo di testa che ha consentito ai rossoneri di pareggiare i conti al Pireo, Alphadjo Cisse è diventato il primo giocatore del Milan a raggiungere la doppia cifra in questa stagione: sei reti in campionato, quattro in Europa League. Curiosamente, l’ultima è arrivata da centravanti, ruolo nel quale il classe 2006 ha giocato nella ripresa della gara con l’Olympiacos, dopo l’uscita dal campo di Goncalo Ramos. Una soluzione alla quale Ruben Amorim era ricorso anche domenica con il Frosinone, ottenendo lo stesso risultato. Acquistato a febbraio dal Verona e lasciato fino a fine stagione al Catanzaro, Cisse sta avendo un rendimento che probabilmente neanche la dirigenza rossonera si sarebbe aspettata. E che dovrebbe tenere in conto.

Qualche giorno fa, Ruben Amorim ha spiegato la differenza tra i due giocatori, sottolineando come Cisse abbia la possibilità di arrivare in area con gli inserimenti da dietro, mentre Ramos dipende molto di più dalla capacità della squadra di servirlo. Tutto vero, ma nelle ultime due partite il ventenne italiano ha dimostrato di saper occupare l’area anche partendo da una posizione più avanzata. Sa far tutto, e tutto bene. Con lui da centravanti, peraltro, il tecnico portoghese può schierare contemporaneamente Hutchinson, Pulisic e Moreira, tutti protagonisti della rimonta in Grecia. Una combinazione offensiva che meriterebbe qualche verifica in più, magari dal primo minuto.

Le parole di Gerry Cardinale su Endrick hanno alimentato le aspettative dei tifosi rossoneri in vista del mercato invernale. L’eventuale partenza in prestito di Francesco Camarda - oltre ai pochi gol di Ramos - giustificherebbe numericamente l’arrivo di un altro attaccante, ma la crescita di Cisse dovrebbe incidere anche sulle valutazioni della società in termini di investimenti. Il Milan ha esigenze piuttosto evidenti in altri settori del campo, a partire dalla difesa: difficile soddisfarle tutte a gennaio. Il rovescio della medaglia è che un altro fromboliere aumenterebbe le ambizioni. Ma, forse, prima di destinare una parte consistente del budget a un nuovo centravanti, varrebbe la pena capire fino a che punto non possa esserlo il suo attuale miglior marcatore, e soprattutto non si rischi di fare ombra alla vera rivelazione italiana di questa stagione.

#2101. Man Utd defender can 'pack his bags' in January as key Mauricio Pochettino transfer decision looms

By Lewis Blain | 25 Nov 2026 08:07

Noussair Mazraoui could be heading towards the Manchester United exit door when the January transfer window opens.

AC Milan are considering a move for the Morocco international as they look to strengthen their defensive options under former Red Devils boss Ruben Amorim.

However, any potential departure will depend on Mauricio Pochettino's assessment of the 29-year-old over the coming weeks.

Mazraoui has experienced mixed fortunes since joining United from Bayern Munich, with injury problems restricting him to fewer than 1,000 Premier League minutes last season.

His fitness has improved this term, and he has featured six times in the league, partly because of injuries to United's other full-back options.

However, his long-term future at Old Trafford is now uncertain, with AC Milan reportedly keeping tabs on his situation.

According to reports in Italy (via Sport Witness), Milan have discussed Mazraoui's profile with their transfer team as they consider defensive reinforcements under Amorim.

The Portuguese coach has regularly used a back four this season and could view the United defender as an attractive option because of his ability to operate on either side.

There is reportedly a belief that Mazraoui could 'pack his bags in January', with the Serie A giants potentially open to pursuing a deal.

However, everything hinges on Pochettino's plans for the player. The Argentine is yet to make a final decision, with the next few weeks expected to help determine whether Mazraoui is allowed to leave.

United have several areas of the squad requiring attention, and selling Mazraoui could provide Pochettino with additional funds to work with in January.

The Argentine is believed to have a budget of just £50m, with the club expected to make only two additions unless significant circumstances change.

With central defence, left-back and striker all potential priorities, every extra contribution to the transfer kitty could prove valuable.

Mazraoui has not consistently established himself in one position at Old Trafford, and a departure could benefit all parties if Milan are prepared to offer a reasonable fee.

United should not allow sentiment to get in the way of a sensible decision. If Pochettino does not see Mazraoui as an important part of his plans, cashing in during January could help fund reinforcements elsewhere.

There is still a decision to be made, but with multiple positions needing attention, every little helps.

Gerry Cardinale gave his thoughts after watching his AC Milan side run riot against Frosinone at San Siro this evening.

It has been yet another few days where the Milan owner has made his presence felt. After landing in Italy, he was at Milanello and Casa Milan on Saturday for a series of important meetings with the management and the squad, and then he watched first hand as the Rossoneri won tonight.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

An own-goal from Gabriele Calvani got the scoring going, before Goncalo Ramos, Ardon Jashari, Alphadjo Cisse and Christian Pulisic all scored in the 5-1 rout of Frosinone. It could have been more in truth, but it was another display of the level this team can play at.

Cardinale was interviewed by Michael Cuomo of TeleLombardia after the final whistle having made his way down from the stands, and his comments were relayed by MilanNews.

“Well, I’m never satisfied, but the results speak for themselves. The boys were fantastic tonight. I hope you’re happy. I hope the fans are happy. I think the team is coming together really well.

“It’s a game, let’s keep it under control, but I’m really proud of the boys. This is a team. It’s a team that wants to win until the end.”

“Look, it’s November. We still have a long way to go. But we want to see improvements in every match. This weekend was very interesting if you look at the kind of results we’ve had. You can see that something is changing in Serie A.

“I was very happy to see us not just win, but win by that margin. And so, we have to build on that, but as Amorim says, every match is something to build on, and we have a long way to go. I personally still expect us to compete for the Scudetto, but it’s a long way off. One match at a time.

“Now the focus is on the Europa League. Yes, and as Ruben said, we have to do both. European competitions are important. Everything is a test. If you want to win a Scudetto, you have to do it all the way, and that’s what we’ll do.”

“Again: January is just another stage. As you know, the January transfer window is complicated. You have to be disciplined. You can’t just jump into something randomly. The only thing that will happen under my ownership is that every time we play in the transfer market, we’ll play to win.

“That’s the theme. That’s the theme I said at the beginning of this year. So, if we make a move in any transfer window, January or June, it will be a move that will count.”

#2092. Amorim proud of ‘intelligent, courageous’ Milan, world-class Rabiot and Ramos

Ruben Amorim insisted that he and his AC Milan team are not getting carried away despite hammering Frosinone, and are simply focusing on the next game.

Milan were in a rampant mood tonight at San Siro, as they put five goals past Massimiliano Alvini’s side and it could have been more in truth. Goncalo Ramos, Ardon Jashari, Alphadjo Cisse and Christian Pulisic all scored, in addition to an own-goal that opened the scoring.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

It was important that the Rossoneri build on their victory over Genoa before the break, especially given that Lazio, Roma, Como and Juventus all won earlier in the round. In addition to that, several individuals shone and the team showed the levels that they are capable of.

Now, the challenge is to follow it up and get back on course in the Europa League in Greece on Thursday night. Amorim spoke to Sky after the final whistle to reflect on what was a very positive night for his side, with his words relayed by MilanNews.

Is that the best Milan performance since you have been here?

“It’s a good feeling, but it’s not just the result: it’s also about consistency throughout the 90 minutes of the match, but now we’re thinking about the next one. It’s always like that at this club, always thinking about the next one.

“Tonight we played well, we were intelligent, courageous, and it showed towards the end of the match. We were tired, yet Frosinone continued to make transitions, but we managed to control them well.

“We could have even scored more goals. Perhaps in the middle of the first half we struggled a little and we need to improve in one-on-one situations defensively, but anyway, it was a good night for us tonight.”

Are you happy for Gonçalo Ramos? How is he?

“We can’t lose any more players. All our players can play every game, but we have little time to recover for the Europa League, so we can’t risk anything. It was better to take him off.

“I imagine he wasn’t happy, but I’m still happy for him, for his goal, especially for the 2-0. It was a goal that made the difference in a match against a team that is very strong in one-on-one transitions.”

Is Rabiot one of the best you’ve ever coached?

“I don’t want to say he could have had a better career because he played at PSG. Now he’s here at Milan and we want him to stay.

“Obviously, he’s a player who can play as a number 10, as an attacking midfielder, he can also play as a number 8, he can also play as a slightly deeper playmaker, even if he doesn’t like it.

“He’s definitely one of the best players I’ve ever coached, he’s one of the best midfielders in Europe right now.”

“The first thing I’ll say is that we have to win the match: if we don’t, then it will be really difficult to qualify for the next stage. Then I think that with Rabiot and Pulisic’s characteristics of playing between the lines, when they can’t play, we have to play and approach the game differently.

“But that’s not an excuse. I said the same things against Ferencváros: we should have played a different game and gotten a different result, but what I’m trying to say is that if we don’t win the match, it will be really difficult and things will go badly for us.

“Then, if we exit the Europa League, we’ll only play once a week and many players won’t play enough. However, I think that if we stay in Europe, we can play more and everyone will continue to grow.

“Furthermore, if we don’t qualify, we will fail to achieve an important objective: so that’s why we have to start the match differently than Benfica and Ferencváros. We’ll see.”

What can you tell us about Cisse as a striker?

“I think he can play very well in that role, in that position. Tonight I chose Cissé to play in that position and not Camarda because against Frosinone, one-on-one, Cissé has these characteristics more. Tonight the goal wasn’t to keep possession, but to run and attack the space.

“Cissé is better at this than Camarda. We wanted to make more transitions than Frosinone, so it was a choice dictated by the player’s characteristics, but I think Cissé is good both as an attacking midfielder and as a central striker, and not just as a winger.”

He literally explained why he chose Cisse over Camarda to sub on.

#2090. Real Madrid: “Something is happening, watch out!” – Thierry Henry impressed by Mourinho's emerging side

By Axel Clody | 25 Nov 2026 09:50

Real Madrid thrashed PSV Eindhoven 5-1 in the Champions League on Tuesday night and are growing in strength this season, thanks to the work of Jose Mourinho and the form of several players.

Thierry Henry's words on CBS on Tuesday night sum up the feeling that is starting to emerge around Real Madrid:

'Real Madrid played an exceptional game. Playing like that, they are serious contenders for the Champions League. They are improving enormously from week to week. Something is going on at Real Madrid, watch out!'

After a start to the season marked by some turbulence, Real Madrid appear to have found their cruising speed. The spectacular win over PSV Eindhoven (5-1) in the Champions League confirmed that impression. Mourinho's side have just strung together a tenth consecutive victory in all competitions and have a perfect record in the Champions League, with five wins from five games. Above all, Real Madrid seem to have taken a step forward in their ability to turn games around.

The script against the Dutch side was far from straightforward. Trailing at half-time after a particularly worrying first half, Real Madrid looked a completely different team after the break. It was a reaction that bore the signature of their manager, who made three changes at half-time.

The other big positive of the evening was Jude Bellingham. Left on the bench at kick-off, the Englishman made a sensational entrance at half-time, to the point of transforming his side. His work rate, ability to get forward and influence between the lines immediately gave Real Madrid more options. His superb goal, scored after a remarkable piece of skill, rewarded a top-class performance.

Thierry Henry, again, was full of admiration:

'(Jude) Bellingham is incredible, the way he plays. Wearing the number 5 at Real Madrid is not easy, but he does it with astonishing ease. His personality is extraordinary. I cannot believe that some people doubted him in his own country. But he has silenced the sceptics with his current performances and proved to them that they were wrong at the World Cup. He always delivers with his attitude and his play.'

Beyond his statistics, with seven goals and five assists in 18 appearances, Bellingham brings an extra dimension to Real Madrid's midfield. Mourinho therefore has a player capable of turning a game, but also of giving a real identity to the side's attacking play.

The Englishman's performance should not, however, overshadow Mourinho's decisions. At half-time, the Portuguese made a bold call with three changes: Bellingham, Ibrahima Konate and Dean Huijsen replaced Aurelien Tchouameni, Antonio Rudiger and Eder Militao. It was a spectacular reshuffle, particularly at centre-back, where the two starters were punished after a difficult first half.

But there was also a desire to rebalance the side behind this choice. By installing Arda Guler and Bernardo Silva in midfield, Mourinho prioritised technical control and ball circulation. Real Madrid were able to push their block up, press higher and regain control of the game. The manager also explained that he used several video clips during the break to correct his players' defensive and attacking mistakes.

'Mourinho is building a fantastic team. Week after week, we see them improving. They lost to Atletico Madrid and we thought the season was over, but they managed to pull themselves together by beating Barcelona' - Carragher said.

The former Liverpool player's comments reflect an enthusiasm that now goes beyond the result of a single match. With a manager capable of changing the course of a game, a rediscovered Bellingham and a side gaining in coherence, Real Madrid are starting to look like credible contenders for the European title. At the same time, Paris Saint-Germain have lost two attacking options in Desire Doue and Khvicha Kvaratskhelia.

#2089. Southampton vs Middlesbrough - prediction, team news, lineups

By Darren Plant | 25 Nov 2026 10:10

Southampton and Middlesbrough square off at St Mary's on Friday night in their first showdown since the conclusion of the Spygate saga.

While the visitors sit at the top of the Championship table, the Saints are just seven points adrift of opponents who are now viewed as one of their fierce rivals.

More than six months on from Tonda Eckert being found guilty of launching a spying mission on a Middlesbrough training session ahead of their playoff semi-final, Southampton have moved on from the controversy that rocked English football.

While Eckert will accept the attention that Friday's game attracts, he will remain focused on trying to secure promotion to the Premier League.

Despite being docked four points as part of their punishment from the Football Association, Southampton can move to within four points of Boro with victory on the South coast.

That said, the Saints have struggled for consistency of late, only collecting five points from four games and conceding a late equaliser to draw 1-1 at Sheffield United on Tuesday night.

Nevertheless, Southampton have been sensational at their home ground. Not only have 22 points being accumulated from a possible 24, they have scored 22 goals and conceded just five.

As for Middlesbrough, they make the long trip south having collected 12 points from eight fixtures, albeit only prevailing on three occasions.

Eight goals had been scored in back-to-back away successes at Stoke City and Bolton Wanderers, yet Kim Hellberg's side have failed to score in the last two such contests versus Wrexham and Watford.

However, 2-0 and 2-1 successes over Bristol City and Swansea City at the Riverside Stadium have ensured that Boro enter this game in top spot with 34 goals from their 17 matches.

Will Lankshear's return from a minor injury may also prove key. Despite being limited to a 62-minute outing versus Swansea, he netted his 14th Championship goal of the season.

Eckert will recall a number of his Southampton regulars for this contest, with Jack Stephens, Ryan Manning and Finn Azaz among those who will start.

James Bree may also feature on the back of his 30-minute outing versus Sheffield United after a minor injury, but Leo Scienza is likely to be named on the substitutes' bench at best after missing the last two matches.

Centre-back Keven Schlotterbeck will be sidelined until December through injury.

As for Boro, Sebastian Berhalter is expected to return in central midfield, with Myles Peart-Harris potentially being used further forward.

Providing that Lankshear shows no ill-effects from Tuesday's game, he should start, with David Strelec likely dropping down to the bench to accommodate an extra midfielder.

Despite concerns over an ankle injury, Neto Borges came through 90 minutes versus Swansea and seems in line to be selected at left-back.

Peretz; Roerslav, Abbott, Stephens, Manning; Downes, Ward-Prowse; Matsuki, Azaz, Dobbin; Larin

Vitek; Brittain, Phillips, Malanda, Borges; Morris, Berhalter; Cozier-Duberry, Peart-Harris, McGree; Lankshear

Although Boro have returned from the November international break with back-to-back wins, we cannot ignore the intensity that these two teams will play at during this contest. That, and home advantage, may favour Southampton, leading us to predict a narrow victory for Eckert's side in a game that will include plenty of cautions.

#2088. Mattarella ricorda Paolo Rossi: "Il suo esempio oggi è al fianco di chi soffre"

Il Presidente della Repubblica, Sergio Mattarella, in occasione della cerimonia inaugurale di Casa Pablito della Paolo Rossi Foundation, ha inviato il seguente messaggio:

"Con l’inaugurazione della prima Casa Pablito presso l’IRCCS Ospedale Sacro Cuore Don Calabria di Negrar, la memoria di Paolo Rossi, indelebilmente associata alle sue straordinarie gesta calcistiche e al trionfo italiano nei Mondiali del 1982 in Spagna, si rinnova nella forma più alta, quella del sostegno e della solidarietà nei confronti di chi lotta contro il cancro.

Quanti si trovano ad affrontare prove così dure e dolorose, oltre a terapie efficaci, chiedono ascolto, vicinanza e la certezza di non essere lasciati soli in momenti di dolore e di preoccupazione, per sé stessi e per i propri cari. La struttura inaugurata oggi, nell’ambito di un’iniziativa benefica che i promotori intendono sviluppare in Italia e poi all’estero, si ispira dichiaratamente a un modello di umanizzazione delle cure. Facilitare l’accoglienza del paziente e della sua famiglia accanto ai luoghi di ricovero ospedaliero, risparmiando loro fatiche e l’onere di coprire talvolta lunghe distanze, contribuisce a mantenere la persona al centro del percorso terapeutico, riconoscendone la dignità anche nei momenti di maggiore fragilità. In questo approccio sono così richiamati i valori della nostra Costituzione a tutela della salute, fondamentale diritto dell'individuo e interesse della collettività.

Alla Paolo Rossi Foundation, che ha voluto e promosso questo progetto, e a quanti hanno contribuito a realizzarlo - medici, operatori, volontari e sostenitori - rivolgo un sentito apprezzamento. Paolo Rossi è stato amato in Italia e nel mondo per il suo talento, ma ancor più per la semplicità, l’umiltà e l’umanità con le quali ha saputo viverlo. È questo esempio, anche nella malattia, più ancora delle sue vittorie sportive, trova oggi una nuova modalità di manifestarsi concretamente al fianco di chi soffre".

#2086. Lazio, "Il nostro Natale insieme": il comunicato di Lotito

Per festeggiare il Natale, quest'anno la Lazio ha pensato ad un evento differente rispetto alla consueta cena

Per festeggiare il Natale, quest'anno la Lazio ha pensato ad un evento differente rispetto alla consueta cena, che si svolgeva di solito a Spazio Novecento.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Come si legge dal comunicato della Lazio con la lettera di Lotito riportata dal Corriere dello Sport, quest'anno l'invito per i tesserati della Lazio è per sabato 19 dicembre alle 15.00 per celebrare la messa di Natale. Inoltre verranno destinate risorse al sostegno di alcune realtà della nostra città che ogni giorno si impegnano ad aiutare le persone e le famiglie che vivono situazioni di difficoltà”.

A tutti i tesserati, dipendenti e collaboratori della S.S. Lazio

il Natale è soprattutto un momento per stare insieme, ritrovarsi e ricordare ciò che conta davvero.

Quest’anno abbiamo pensato di scambiarci gli auguri in un modo diverso, più semplice, ma forse proprio per questo ancora più significativo.

Sabato 19 dicembre 2026, alle ore 15:00, ci ritroveremo nella parrocchia San Tommaso Moro, di Don Gabriele, nel quartiere San Lorenzo a Roma, per celebrare insieme la Santa Messa di Natale. Vorrei che fosse un momento per tutti noi, per sentirci parte di una comunità che ogni giorno condivide lavoro, impegno, responsabilità, gioie e difficoltà. Perché la Lazio, prima ancora di essere una società sportiva, è fatta di persone.

Quest’anno abbiamo anche deciso di vivere lo spirito del Natale attraverso un gesto concreto di solidarietà, destinando risorse al sostegno di alcune realtà della nostra città che ogni giorno si impegnano ad aiutare le persone e le famiglie che vivono situazioni di difficoltà.

Ci sono tante persone e tante famiglie che stanno attraversando momenti complicati. Credo che il modo migliore per vivere lo spirito del Natale sia anche quello di pensare a loro, rinunciando a qualcosa per noi per poter offrire qualcosa agli altri.

Non sarà un gesto che risolverà tutti i problemi, ma sarà il nostro modo di esserci, concretamente.

Mi piacerebbe davvero che il 19 dicembre fossimo tutti presenti. Non per un appuntamento formale, ma per il piacere di ritrovarci, guardarci negli occhi, scambiarci gli auguri e condividere un momento che appartiene a tutti noi.

Perché essere una comunità significa anche questo: sentirsi parte di qualcosa e sapere che, insieme, possiamo fare del bene.

Vi aspetto e, fin da ora, auguro a voi e alle vostre famiglie un Natale sereno, con l’auspicio che il nuovo anno possa portare salute, pace e soddisfazioni.

Parrocchia San Tommaso Moro Via dei Ramni, 40, 00185 Roma (angolo Via dei Marrucini).

#2085. African Player of the Year 2026: Final shortlist revealed without Ismaila Sarr

By Axel Clody | 25 Nov 2026 11:02

On Tuesday night, in Dakar, it was possible to seriously imagine Ismaila Sarr lifting the African Ballon d'Or. The Court of Arbitration for Sport (CAS) had just confirmed Senegal's triumph at the Africa Cup of Nations, and the Crystal Palace winger saw his candidacy significantly strengthened.

Yet the next day, his name had disappeared from the list. The Confederation of African Football (CAF) selected Sadio Mane and Pape Gueye on the Senegalese side, along with Achraf Hakimi, Brahim Diaz and Ismael Saibari for Morocco.

?????? ?? ??? ???? (???) - ????????? #CAFAwards2026 https://t.co/RcZAogxLza

A winner of the Conference League with Crystal Palace, Sarr also finished as the competition's top scorer with nine goals and was named the best player of the tournament.

At the World Cup, he scored four times, including a goal against Belgium in the round of 32. He has a total of 24 goals and five assists in 53 games in 2026, for club and country. That annual record goes beyond the period assessed by CAF, which ended on 15 September. His European title and his World Cup, however, fell within that window, as did the Africa Cup of Nations triumph, even though its validation only came after the fact.

Pape Gueye scored the goal that won the Africa Cup of Nations for Senegal and features in the team of the tournament. Sadio Mane, the winner in 2019 and 2022, was named the best player of this Africa Cup of Nations. Their places are defensible.

But if the three Senegalese had to be separated over the whole period, Sarr's case, decisive at club level and at the World Cup, appears more complete. He had what it took to aim for first place. Seeing him miss out on the top five is hard to understand.

The CAS ruling revived that hope in Senegal when voting had already closed. CAF had set the end of voting for 20 November, four days before the verdict.

Achraf Hakimi remains the favourite among the five selected. The 2025 winner, the full-back won a second consecutive Champions League with Paris Saint-Germain and reached the World Cup quarter-finals with Morocco.

Brahim Diaz finished as the top scorer at the Africa Cup of Nations with five goals. His missed Panenka in the final remains the most striking image of his tournament, after he had found the net in five games in a row.

Ismael Saibari, meanwhile, scored against Brazil, Scotland and Haiti. He became the first African to score in each of his country's three group games at a single World Cup. Currently injured, the Bayern player can therefore also hope for a crowning at the ceremony on 2 December in Lagos.

Why didn’t you shoot before the first goal?

“I wanted Pulisic to score, but the defender came back quickly. It’s true that I could have shot, but I get more pleasure from providing an assist than from scoring. But I did the assist afterwards.”

“He does a great job. Not everyone understands it, but he does an incredible job. When we get in and score, it’s also credit to us. Then we feel happy when he scores, also because it gives him confidence. Good for us and for him, for everyone.”

#2082. Leeds transfer news: Will Lankshear January hopes shot down by 44-goal striker

By Ben Knapton | 25 Nov 2026 11:33

Former Leeds United striker Brian Deane, speaking exclusively to Sports Mole, has played down the Whites' chances of signing Will Lankshear in the January transfer window.

The former Tottenham Hotspur youngster has taken the Championship by storm since moving to the Riverside Stadium in the summer, scoring 14 goals in 15 second-tier appearances.

Lankshear netted the winner as Middlesbrough beat Swansea City 2-1 on Tuesday night, a result that saw Kim Hellberg's men move one point clear of Wolverhampton Wanderers at the top of the Championship table.

The 21-year-old unsurprisingly sits top of the Championship top scorers charts, three clear of closest challenger Andri Gudjohnsen of Blackburn Rovers in the race for the Golden Boot.

Lankshear's form has inevitably led to speculation surrounding a swift return to the Premier League, with multiple clubs believed to be interested in securing his services, including Leeds.

Former club Tottenham, Arsenal, Brighton & Hove Albion and Newcastle United have also been credited with an interest, although Middlesbrough's £70m asking price could deter suitors.

Now, former Leeds forward Deane - who scored 44 goals in 199 matches for the Whites - has expressed his view that Lankshear should remain at the Riverside Stadium over the winter.

“This is his breakout season, and he is at a good club," Deane told Sports Mole. "Middlesbrough look on course to challenge for promotion, so hopefully he gets there with them.

“He will be ambitious and attract attention from clubs such as Leeds, Brighton and Newcastle. He would be a good addition to a squad. But for his own development, I think he is in a good place at Middlesbrough.”

Lankshear made just six senior appearances for Tottenham before being sold in a £9.8m deal over the summer, and the striker's contract with Middlesbrough does not expire until 2031.

A young striker scoring for fun in the Championship will always attract glances from top-tier clubs, but Lankshear may struggle to force his way into Daniel Farke's first XI right now.

First-choice pairing Dominic Calvert-Lewin and Noah Okafor boast a respectable nine Premier League goals between them in 2026-27, and as long as both are fit, they will spearhead Farke's 3-5-2 system.

Calvert-Lewin is now in the final 24 months of his contract at Elland Road, but the England international is understood to be close to signing a new Leeds deal.

Lukas Nmecha also provides a steady backup option, although he is due to become a free agent in 2027 and may seek a move away in January if his game-time situation does not improve.

However, Nmecha's proposed exit would then open the door for summer signing Jean-Matteo Bahoya to earn more opportunities, so a winter move for Lankshear would make little sense for all involved.

Brian Deane was speaking to Sports Mole on behalf of BetWright.

Il paragone con Robben riguarda dunque Isaksen, mentre il giudizio positivo si estende al gioco dell’intera squadra.

Parlando della Roma, Longhi ha definito inatteso il segnale arrivato dalla squadra. Ha richiamato City-Napoli, partita in cui a suo giudizio aveva prevalso il tatticismo, e l’ha contrapposta alla gara giocata a viso aperto sul campo del PSG, che nelle sue parole domina da due anni in Champions. La Roma, ha osservato, ha affrontato l’impegno come se avesse davanti un’altra squadra di Serie A.

Longhi ha descritto la formazione giallorossa attraverso forza, temperamento, pressing e tecnica. Di Gasperini ha detto che ha giocato sul campo del PSG scegliendo i calciatori da impiegare senza preoccupazioni: per lui è il segnale di una rosa adatta a qualsiasi tipo di impegno.

Sull’Inter il giudizio è diverso: «Ha disputato 17 partite e non si è mai vista l'Inter come lo scorso anno o di due anni fa, per vari motivi. L'Inter rimane forte, però rimane indecifrabile.» Per questo, nel confronto di campionato, Longhi indica più la Roma dei nerazzurri.

#2078. Arsenal transfer news: Italian giants chase Serie A sensation on Gunners' radar

By Axel Clody | 25 Nov 2026 12:04

Major Serie A clubs, namely Napoli, Juventus and AC Milan, but not only them, are keen to sign the league's breakout star. But the price for him is already high, at £34m (€40m).

This winter, many Italian clubs are looking for a centre-forward. According to Gazzetta dello Sport, that is the case for Massimiliano Allegri's Napoli, after Italian Lorenzo Lucca (26) was put on the market. Juventus are in the same situation, as manager Luciano Spalletti has repeated several times recently, while Ruben Amorim's AC Milan have been keen on Endrick (Real Madrid) and Nicolo Tresoldi (Club Brugge) for several weeks.

Como could also look into the matter after Moise Kean's long injury. Inter Milan, for their part, could act in that position in the next summer transfer window, especially as Pio Esposito's stock is soaring.

Robinson ? Pessina ? Varela ? #MonzaFiorentina #SerieAEnilive https://t.co/RAbjku9GHz

However, according to Gazzetta dello Sport, all of these clubs are interested in Gustavo Varela.

The same goes for the Premier League, where Arsenal are reported to be following him, while Manchester United have been loosely linked. Tottenham Hotspur and Brighton and Hove Albion are also said to have made enquiries, although none of the English clubs are believed to have made a formal approach.

Signed by Monza from Benfica's reserve side this summer for £2.1m (€2.5m), the 21-year-old Portuguese is lighting up the season, with 13 goals, including 11 in Serie A, in 12 games in all competitions.

The striker's brilliant backheel goal against Fiorentina (3-0) on Monday did not lower his value. He is under contract until June 2030 with the team currently 14th in Serie A. The question everyone is asking now is whether Varela can leave Monza as early as January 2027.

Gol capolavoro di Varela! ? Raddoppio del Monza ? #MonzaFiorentina #SerieAEnilive #UNROSSOALLAVIOLENZA https://t.co/LRPv0nsSfS

Asked about the subject on Monday, the Portuguese said he is very relaxed about his future:

'I continue to thank Monza for welcoming me. Everything has been incredible so far: I feel very lucky to be part of this team and I hope it continues like this. Transfer rumours are part of football. I am living great moments and I am simply trying to enjoy the present without worrying about the news.'

Unfazed by the rumours, Varela seems above all to want to stay at Monza, at least for the current season. But this winter, could Italian heavyweights or Premier League clubs not make an offer of between £30m and £34m (€35m to €40m), his value according to Gazzetta dello Sport?

That would certainly be enough to rock 14th-placed Monza, with a huge profit made in four months. The battle is on.

#2077. GdS: ‘Turbo Milan’ – how Frosinone were dismantled by five-star counter-attacking display

AC Milan put in one of their best performances under Ruben Amorim so far to sweep aside Frosinone at San Siro.

La Gazzetta dello Sport (seen below) begin a report by writing that Lazio, Roma and Juventus also had fun this weekend, scoring a flurry of goals in their victories, but it is Milan that might just come away from the round 12 feeling best.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Within 20 minutes, the Rossoneri had already blown the game wide open, eventually putting five past their opponents. All of them were spectacular, lightning-fast, and following the same pattern: every single one a counter-attack.

It was a throwback style of football, yet infused with the visionary approach of Amorim, making it both fresh and truly beautiful to watch. There may not be official statistics on this, but conceding from five lethal transitions feels like a record as Frosinone’s strategy certainly went beyond mere self-sabotage.

Yet, on days like this, this Milan – an enigma to its opponents, with fluid movement and interchangeable positioning – are a truly daunting prospect. When Adrien Rabiot, Diego Moreira, Luka Modrić and others perform at such a high level, we see the potential of this side.

The Diavolo have secured five wins in their last six league matches, yet in Europe, they have suffered three defeats in four games. Will Thursday’s clash against Olympiacos help restore the balance?

There was nothing balanced about the game between Milan and Frosinone. It is true that Alvini’s incredibly bold (perhaps even reckless) side tried to go toe-to-toe with their opponents for an hour by attacking, playing long balls, and taking on defenders.

Yet, beyond this emphasis on a specific style of play, one could make more concrete assessments. The coach clearly understood how vital Raimondo is to a team featuring such direct and creative wingers, Ghedjemis on the right and Kvernadze (who was rather ineffective yesterday) on the left.

Raimondo was the perfect focal point for them and without him, things had already gone poorly against Cagliari. Alvini attempted to make adjustments but got it wrong: he abandoned his 4-2-3-1 formation in favour of a back three (Calvani, Monterisi, Cittadini).

All this was undermined by a senseless tendency to push too high, maintaining a defensive line 57 metres from their own goal within a 3-4-3 setup that lacked a true striker, opting instead for a ‘false’ one in Schmid. Every time they lost possession, it resulted in a counter-attack and often a goal.

It might seem odd to start with Frosinone rather than the brilliance of Milan, but there’s no denying that the visitors played their part in the spectacle. This is a match that future opponents will need to study in minute detail, because Milan on the counter are a goal-scoring machine.

Amorim, too, has a knack for springing surprises, though as always the guessing game regarding his actual tactical setup for the day begins the moment the whistle blows. For example, it was not a back four as it appeared on paper.

Instead, the system actually gradually morphed into a 3-5-2, with Rabiot operating as a highly advanced right-sided central midfielder, almost an attacking midfielder. The key lies in the connection between the Frenchman and the stratospheric Moreira, who surged down the centre often.

Keeping everything running at full throttle was the incredible playmaking of Modrić, who also serves as the team’s primary ball-winner. So much for being a ‘pensioner’.

Milan proved flawless in exploiting both structural weaknesses and individual errors. Frosinone’s DNA is defined by a headlong, aggressive approach, but the opening goal – stemming from a sequence on the right flank involving Modrić and Rabiot – ended up as an own goal by Calvani.

Just four minutes in, and the match was effectively decided. While Frosinone usually thrive on quick, darting attacks, here the roles were reversed. Another full-pitch surge, initiated by Ramos winning the ball from Calvani and carried forward by Moreira and Rabiot, left the Portuguese player unmarked to make it 2-0.

Only a reckless back pass from Musah gifted Masini a goal to pull it back to 2-1. In the second half, however, Amorim addressed the one area that looked shaky: he withdrew Bartesaghi – who had been struggling against Ghedjemis – shifting Musah to full-back and pairing Jashari with Modrić.

It paid off immediately: Jashari broke away and fired home the 3-1 following yet another rapid transition. And that wasn’t the only one: Pulisic surged clear on his own after a Frosinone corner (4-1), and Cissé, having just come on, joined the fray at full throttle to add the fifth (5-1).

There were many top performances. Rabiot is like a freight train and deserved a goal. He had to settle for three assists (bringing his total to seven). Moreira is versatile, playing as a wing-back, a central midfielder, or a winger, much like he did for Belgium when they caused Italy so much trouble.

Ramos also moved well outside of his goal. Cissé was an absolute handful: he came on and scorrd, playing as a striker after the Portuguese felt a tweak. He did so with an effortless grace that makes him impossible for his markers to handle.

The end result is that Milan are back in fourth. The competition is fierce, but the Rossoneri are right there in the mix, and eventually, some of the others will stumble. Take Inter, for example. Frosinone need to rethink their strategy while waiting for the return of Raimondo.

They’ve overachieved so far, but now they might need to be a bit more prudent. And what about Milan? If they don’t turn their trip to Athens into a Greek tragedy, perhaps their European campaign can get back on track, too.

#2076. Aston Villa news: Why Unai Emery's immunity from criticism should no longer exist

By Darren Plant | 25 Nov 2026 12:36

Such is Unai Emery's status at Aston Villa that few would be surprised if the redeveloped North Stand, when completed in 2027, is named after the Spaniard.

Taking Villa from relegation candidates in 2022-23 to two Champions League qualifications and a Europa League trophy by the conclusion of 2025-26 arguably deserves that level of recognition.

However, despite the expectation that a contract extension past 2029 will be penned in the near future, it will not necessarily strengthen Emery's, or the club's, position in the long term.

Tuesday's 1-0 defeat at Galatasaray extended Villa's recent winless streak across all competitions to six matches. They have failed to score in four of those games, and given up leads on three occasions.

Emery is no stranger to an extended blip in form. At the start of both 2025-26 and 2026-27, Villa were off the pace. It is to Emery's credit that Villa became brief Premier League title contenders in a matter of months and still finished in fourth position in last season's standings.

Nevertheless, Villa are now enduring their second extended winless period of the season, raising the question of whether the West Midlands outfit are experiencing a blip or a gradual decline under their most successful manager for nearly 45 years?

With 116 wins from 215 matches, silverware, and delivering moments and experiences that will last a lifetime, Emery is etched into the Villa history books as a God.

At the same time, this is now a manager of a team that has only recorded 11 wins from his last 27 games in all competitions, as well as losing on 10 occasions. Just seven wins have come from 19 fixtures in 2026-27.

When Villa defeated Tottenham Hotspur, Newcastle United and Manchester City in relatively quick succession with performances that mixed grit, shrewdness and attacking vigour, the conclusion was that Emery's new-looked team had flicked a switch.

However, Villa have regressed as quickly as they had seemingly improved. When you take into account that Villa had one win in seven between February 1 and March 4, and lost three in a row between April 25 and May 3, this is now the fourth "blip" in less than nine months.

Some of the aforementioned statistics will be comparable with managers at similarly-sized clubs in the Premier League and Europe, yet that usually leads to scrutiny over the job that has been conducted over a specific period. Not talk of a contract until 2030 and beyond being signed.

By modern-day standards, Emery is in the unique position of having high control over which players are signed and sold. He essentially has the keys to the footballing side of the club and Villa's fate on the pitch is solely in his hands.

Yet, a 12-time trophy-winning manager of a team that has only earned fewer Premier League points than Arsenal, Manchester City and Liverpool across a four-year period is frequently telling the media, or anyone who will listen, that Villa should not be competing in the top six, nor for the top-eight positions in the league phase of the Champions League.

Is this an example of Emery trying to reinforce the perception that Villa are underdogs to the so-called Premier League 'big six' because of what is, admittedly, a scandalous PSR scenario, or is it a pre-orchestrated approach to defend his position whenever results do not go Villa's way?

Staying on the subject of things being pre-orchestrated, not enough scrutiny is coming over Villa's and Emery's use of Liverpool's Harvey Elliott and Chelsea's Alejandro Garnacho.

Since the summer of 2025, Villa have included £35m and, allegedly, £43m fees with those clubs to sign the players in question should they achieve a specific appearance threshold. Between them, they have made three starts and 13 substitute outings when spending the majority of the time available for selection.

On Tuesday night, Emery omitted Garnacho - who has accumulated fewer than 100 minutes across seven substitute appearances - from the squad for the Galatasaray match, despite naming one fewer substitute than was permitted. Afterwards, he delivered a frank response over the Argentine's chances of earning future game time.

If Villa do not want to pay vast amounts of money for players, they do not have to. However, are Villa not harming their chances of signing players through similar deals in the future? Why should clubs trust such deals to go through if Emery is choosing not to select such players or is being told not to?

Everything points to Garnacho becoming Elliott 2:0 and the situation deserves some clarity. If an honest assessment cannot be delivered to fans and the media, Emery leaves himself open to persistent questioning and criticism, and it will be warranted.

Of course, the evolving Garnacho saga can change for the better. Excluding Villa's game against Chelsea in December, the winger has seven more chances to feature in 2026.

There can be a rapid upturn in fortunes, too. Villa face Ipswich Town, Everton and Crystal Palace in the Premier League before they welcome Paris Saint-Germain to Villa Park in the Champions League.

However, when Emery is storming down tunnels before the full-time whistle, throwing tantrums in the media and getting increasingly frustrated on the touchline as his team look slow, devoid of ideas and can make 700 passes in a game and not score against a team such as Sunderland, it can be argued that Villa need a change of direction rather than remaining on their current path.

Villa have a deep squad with talent that can earn them success in the future, yet they have too many under-performers or players who may simply be tired even at this stage of the season. Whether that is down to Emery, or too much emphasis being placed on players needing to adapt to the "Emery way" before they earn regular starts, it is open to debate.

Regardless, Villa are currently on a downward trajectory under his leadership, and Emery has questions to answer if he is to remain untouchable at a club that want to hand him a contract extension when they could enter December with just three Premier League wins in 2026-27.

#2075. Sampdoria, il club condanna gli insulti razzisti e territoriali a Insigne, Makoumbou e Mbabu

La Sampdoria prende posizione dopo gli episodi verificatisi sugli spalti dello stadio Euganeo durante la sfida contro il Padova. Il club blucerchiato ha diffuso una nota ufficiale per condannare gli insulti a sfondo territoriale e razzista rivolti a tre suoi calciatori e chiedere che vengano individuati i responsabili.

L’U.C. Sampdoria condanna fermamente gli episodi di insulti a sfondo territoriale e razzista verificatisi sugli spalti dello stadio “Euganeo” in occasione della gara Padova-Sampdoria, disputata mercoledì 25 novembre, ai danni dei calciatori Lorenzo Insigne, Antoine Makoumbou e Kevin Mbabu.

Il club esprime la propria piena solidarietà e vicinanza ai propri calciatori, ribadendo con fermezza che comportamenti di questo genere sono inaccettabili e non devono trovare spazio nel mondo del calcio.

Nel rispetto del Calcio Padova, al quale non viene attribuita alcuna responsabilità, la società auspica che vengano effettuati i necessari accertamenti, in collaborazione con la Lega B, affinché i responsabili siano individuati e sanzionati.

L’U.C. Sampdoria continuerà a seguire con attenzione gli sviluppi della vicenda, nella convinzione che il rispetto e la tutela della dignità delle persone debbano essere valori imprescindibili dello sport.

#2074. Cisse discusses desire to add assists, not even being at 50% and why he must thank Gabbia

Alphadjo Cisse has revealed that he would like to add more assists to his game, as well as why he has Matteo Gabbia to thank above all.

Milan put on a ruthless display at San Siro on Sunday night, beating Frosinone 5-1 to move back into the top four. Calvani’s own-goal started the rout, while Goncalo Ramos, Ardon Jashari and Christian Pulisic all scored.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Cisse got his name on the scoresheet again just minutes after coming on, netting his ninth goal of the season. Diego Moreira set him up with a nice through ball in transition, and though the offside flag went up initially, VAR overturned it to award the goal.

After the game, Cisse spoke to both Sky and DAZN to give his thoughts on what was another very positive night for him personally and for the team. His comments are via MilanNews.

“I definitely always try to contribute in all positions, and I definitely prefer to play in my own position, but I’m always available in all roles. Which position? I won’t say (laughs).”

Where can you still grow and what does Amorim ask of you?

“I have to improve in many other areas. As always, scoring is always great. I’d also like to add a few assists for some of my team-mates, but the most important thing was always to bring home three points, and today we did that.”

Is there a team-mate you have to thank in particular?

“I definitely think Gabbia, because I was initially coming from Serie B, and settling into that kind of environment is never easy. But I had teammates who welcomed me very well, him especially, so for me everything was much easier.”

We also see you as a possible centre-forward…

“I’ll tell you the truth, come on, I’ll tell you. I prefer to play in the attacking midfield because, as I’ve always done, I always want to try to make plays, to entertain people. But maybe, who knows, I’ll also play as a striker, I don’t know. Let’s see…”

“At first I thought it was. Then, as they were checking it a bit, I thought it wasn’t.”

You’re Milan’s top scorer. Will we see a bicycle kick soon?

“The overhead kick used to be my specialty, but now it’s not. Let’s hope it happens. It’s always a great feeling to score, I want to add a few assists too. The important thing was the three points, and we got them.”

How far along are you in your development?

“Right now, I’m not even halfway there. Maybe even lower. There’s still a lot of work to do to get there. But I’m in the right place, and hopefully we can get there.”

Have you ever thought about what would have happened if you had gone to PSV?

“Better not to think about it. Let’s just say that I’ve always made the right decisions with my family, and thanks go to them, to the coach, to my team-mates, and I’m in the right place and on the right path.”

“Progress is the right word, because we’ve changed coaches, and it’s never easy. We’re a great team, with many champions. We still have a lot of work to do. Milan always has our backs, and that’s very important to me.”

#2073. Venezia vs. Bologna - prediction, team news, lineups

By Seye Omidiora | 25 Nov 2026 12:37

Venezia head into their matchday 13 encounter with in-form Bologna seeking a third league victory in four matches at Stadio Pier Luigi Penzo.

The Europe-chasing visitors sit 11 places above the Veneto-based club in the Serie A table but will be wary of the Winged Lions' recent improvement in front of their supporters.

On the surface, Venezia's decision to replace Giovanni Stroppa with Davide Nicola has made little difference to their results, with the Lagunari sitting bottom of the league.

However, a closer look suggests that the home supporters can expect a battling performance when Bologna travel to Venice on Friday.

Still the only Serie A side yet to draw this term, the promoted outfit have suffered 10 defeats in 12 matches, including four since Nicola's appointment in October.

Nonetheless, all six of their points have come from their last three matches at Stadio Pier Luigi Penzo, where they have beaten Napoli (1-0) and Udinese (2-1) either side of a 2-0 defeat to Inter Milan.

That loss to the defending champions came courtesy of own goals from Matias Moreno and Redouane Halhal in the final 10 minutes, with the bottom-placed side attempting 12 shots to Inter's 16 and creating two clear-cut chances to the Nerazzurri's three.

Although three matches represent a small sample, those recent performances in Venice suggest that the Lagunari are slowly turning the tide at home, giving Bologna reason for caution ahead of Friday's trip.

Raffaele Palladino's Rossoblu know all about upturns in form, having experienced their own resurgence since Domenico Tedesco was dismissed in mid-September following a winless start to the campaign.

A 1-0 defeat to Napoli at the Maradona proved the final straw for the hierarchy in Bologna, with Palladino inheriting a Felsinei side that had collected just one point from an available 12 across four matches.

Sitting 16th at the time and outside the bottom three only on goal difference, the Rossoblu have since collected 17 points from a possible 24 under Palladino to climb to ninth on 18 points, just three behind sixth-placed Juventus.

Only Lazio (22), AC Milan (19) and Roma (18) have earned more points during that period, underlining Bologna's resurgence since Tedesco's departure as they target a sixth victory in nine league matches.

The travelling supporters will therefore back their in-form side to overcome Nicola's men, although the result may be less straightforward than some anticipate given the Lagunari's steady improvement on home turf.

Toma Basic (muscle), Thierry Correia (thigh), Pasquale Mazzocchi (muscle), Andrea Adorante (hernia), Marin Sverko (hip) and Bartol Franjic (shoulder) are all considered unlikely to feature in gameweek 13.

No player has scored more than twice for the division's lowest scorers, with Akor Adams and Antoine Hainaut netting two apiece to account for half of the Winged Lions' eight league goals.

While Bologna have no individual producing spectacular numbers, they boast a broader spread of match-winners, with Federico Bernardeschi, Samuel Mbangula, Riccardo Orsolini and Tommaso Pobega all scoring twice.

Although Juan Miranda, Nicolo Cambiaghi and Jens Odgaard have yet to score, their respective returns of five, three and two assists underline their importance to the Rossoblu.

However, Bologna must manage without Miranda, who is sidelined alongside Jay Enem (Achilles), Oussama El Azzouzi (thigh) and Emil Holm (thigh).

Stankovic; Halhal, Moreno, Juan Jesus; Hainaut, Kike Perez, Sohm, Busio, Haps; Lauberbach, Adams

Skorupski; Darmian, Casale, Theate; Zortea, Ferguson, Pobega, Cambiaghi; Bernardeschi, Orsolini; Odgaard

Venezia's victories over Napoli and Udinese suggest that their lowly position does not tell the whole story, particularly after they made Inter work for their points in Venice.

However, Bologna's 17-point return from eight matches under Palladino reflects a more sustained improvement, and the visitors should have enough attacking variety to edge Friday's encounter.

#2071. Levante vs Racing de Santander - prediction, team news, lineups

By Matt Law | 25 Nov 2026 12:48, Last updated: 25 Nov 2026 12:50

Racing de Santander will be bidding to continue their excellent run of form when they head to Estadi Ciutat de Valencia on Friday evening to tackle Levante.

Levante just about lead the overall head-to-head record between these two sides, winning 11 of their 30 meetings in all competitions, suffering 10 defeats in the process.

Levante have a record of two wins, six draws and five defeats from their 13 league matches this season, with 12 points leaving them down in 15th spot in the La Liga table.

The Frogs have been an entertaining watch at home this season, scoring 14 times and conceding 13, boasting a record of two wins, two draws and two defeats.

Luis Castro's side will enter this match off the back of a 1-1 draw with Elche, while they also drew 0-0 with Celta Vigo before the November international break, with their last victory coming at home to Athletic Bilbao on October 21.

Levante finished 16th in Spain's top flight last season, so there is clear room for improvement in 2026-27, but they have found it difficult to win matches so far this term.

The Frogs actually beat Racing 3-1 in the last match between the two teams in April 2025, but two of their last four meetings have been won by the latter.

Racing, who are the reigning Segunda Division champions, have been one of the stories of the season in Spain's top flight, impressing hugely on their return to La Liga.

The Green and Whites boast a record of five wins, two draws and six defeats from their 13 league matches, which has left them seventh in the table on 17 points.

Jose Alberto's side have actually conceded 32 times in La Liga this season, which is comfortably the worst record in the division, but they have found the back of the net on 20 occasions - the sixth-best attacking record at this stage.

Racing will enter this match off the back of a 3-2 win over Real Sociedad, and they have now been victorious in three of their last four in the league, and four of their last five in all competitions.

The Green and Whites have struggled away from home this season, though, losing five of their six league matches, only managing to pick up three points.

Racing de Santander form (all competitions):

Levante lost Jeremy Toljan to an injury in the early stages of their clash with Elche last time out, and the defender is set to miss out on Friday.

The hosts have not reported any further problems, so aside from Nacho Perez coming in for Toljan at right-back, it could be the same side that started the team's last game.

Roger Brugue has scored five times and registered one assist in 13 appearances this season, and the 30-year-old is again set for a spot in the starting side.
