Tuttosport meanwhile gave Rabiot an 8, distributing 7.5s instead to Moreira, Modric, Pulisic and the coach Amorim. Scores of 7 went to Jashari, Musah, Ramos and Cisse. Gila, De Winter and Diawara got given a 6.5, and everyone else a 6.

#2066. Rabiot and Ruben’s rampant Rossoneri: All the key stats from AC Milan 5-1 Frosinone

AC Milan produced their best attacking display of the season on Sunday night as they put five goals past Frosinone at San Siro.

An own-goal from Gabriele Calvani early on set the tone, with Goncalo Ramos, Ardon Jashari, Alphadjo Cisse and Christian Pulisic all netting in the rout. The ruthless counter-attacking display moves Milan back inside the top four, after wins from Como, Roma and Lazio earlier in the round.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

The match stats make for interesting reading. Milan edged possession with 54%, generating 2.50 Expected Goals – so scoring double what they created – but also having five big chances which matches the number of goals scored.

Frosinone remarkably had more shots in total (15 to 12), but they were more wasteful with only three on target to the Rossoneri’s eight. The visitors also racked up nine corners, the most Milan have conceded all season.

📊 The stats from the win against Frosinone. pic.twitter.com/ERVTj98m9Y

— MilanData📊 (@acmilandata) November 22, 2026

Only Nereo Rocco’s (29) and Lajos Czeizler’s (31) Milan sides scored more goals than Ruben Amorim’s (28) in their first 12 matches in charge in Serie A.

Adrien Rabiot is the first Milan midfielder to be involved in at least one goal (including goals and assists) in seven consecutive Serie A appearances since Kaká in 2004, and he became the first midfielder in the last 20 years of Serie A with more than 10 goals in his first 10 games in a single season.

With his sixth goal in his 10th Serie A appearance for Milan at 20 years and 31 days, Alphadjo Cisse became the second-youngest Rossoneri player to reach this milestone in his first 10 appearances in the three-points-for-a-win era, after Alexandre Pato , who achieved it at 18 years and 189 days.

No team has scored more goals than Milan in the first 15 minutes of play in the five major European leagues currently underway (7, like Barcelona and Chelsea).

Milan are now unbeaten in all seven meetings against Frosinone in Serie A (W5 D2): among the teams against which they have never lost in the competition, the Rossoneri have played more games only against Pisa (14 – six also against Crotone and Lecco).

Milan have won five of their last six league games (L1), scoring at least two goals in each, after failing to win three of their previous four (D3). The last time they scored more than one goal in a run of Serie A matches was between September 2020 and January 2021, when they managed 15 games.

#2061. Amorim explains dislike for ‘AmorimBall’ term and what leaves him ‘really worried’ about Milan

Ruben Amorim had some interesting comments after the Frosinone win, including why he is not a fan of a term that is going viral.

Milan put on a five-star display at San Siro on Sunday night as they routed that Canarini. All five of their goals came via flowing counter-attacks, which has led to the tag ‘AmorimBall’ doing the rounds on social media, with many believing things are starting to truly click.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

The head coach is keeping his feet firmly on the ground though, as he spoke after the game about the need to find consistency both within matches but also from game-to-game. In this sense, Thursday’s trip to face Olympiacos is another big test.

Amorim was present in the press conference room at San Siro following the win to answer questions from journalists, with his comments relayed by MilanNews.

“I don’t like that expression. I think we play games to win, we want to dominate the opponent. I don’t think we succeeded today: every game has its own story, and today it was man-on-man.

“Today we didn’t have to prepare much: the idea was simply to give the ball to Pulisic, to Moreira, and then exploit the combinations of these players, the understandings they already have, and conquer space.

“Today you saw that we didn’t spend much time near the opponent’s penalty area and we scored five goals, because the space wasn’t there, it was forty meters away to attack on the run.

“That’s why Cisse came on to play as a striker and not, for example, Camarda: because there was space behind him and Cisse is very good at that. So, I think we’ve played very good games in the past, but today it was just a question of the quality of the players, of one-on-ones, of reading the game.

“Today it wasn’t a question of the coach, I think, but of the players. And they, since it was man-on-man, just had to do what they can do. when they’re so good: beating the man and opening up space. So today the credit goes entirely to the players.”

“I watched the France game and I think he’s one of the best players on the team. So I think his importance is paramount. You can see there’s rotation, there’s a lot of talent. France has a lot of talent, but Rabiot is always there. There’s a reason for that.

“And then, if you look at the way France play, with Mbappé, with the way all these players position themselves on the pitch, sometimes with four players with Olise as the No. 10, he has to stay there to play a different kind of game.

“With us, he’s the player who has to push the team forward, he’s the decisive element. In France, however, he has a different job to do. So it depends more on his role in the national team and his role at Milan.

“This changes his way of interpreting the game, and it’s further proof of how Rabiot can play by adapting to the team he’s in. For me, therefore, he’s one of the best midfielders in Europe. I hope his contract renewal comes soon.”

“Today’s match wasn’t just about tactics. We saw changes in position, one-on-one situations with plenty of space: it seemed like a crazy game. What I felt against Genoa and in the other matches was that we were too far away, we didn’t reach the final third of the pitch.

“We’re not like the big teams: if you look at the big teams, like Juventus, they might struggle to get results, but you see the volume they produce. For us, it’s a bit of the opposite, so I’m really worried that, if we don’t improve, we won’t be able to maintain this number of points.

“Today, however, was a step forward: we saw many players attacking the depth, something we hadn’t seen against Ferencvaros. In the other matches, we’d managed the ball well, but today we needed pace and runs.

“We need to be able to play both games within the same game, but today was clearly the type of game we needed to win.”

Laurent Courtois has seen his side score three or more goals in four of their last five matches versus MLS opposition, while posting a clean sheet in three of those instances, making them an appealing option to choose on the UK betting sites

The Crew exposed gaps in the Miami squad, scoring on three of their five targeted efforts last weekend and putting the Herons on the ropes heading into game two.

This is only the second time they are up 1-0 since this best-of-three first-round format was introduced, with this team advancing past Atlanta United in three games on the only previous occasion in which they were in this position in 2023.

Only once since their 2015 MLS Cup final defeat versus the Portland Timbers have they lost a post-season match at home, with the New York Red Bulls blanking them 1-0 in game one of that opening round series in 2024.

Friday will mark the second time the Crew can eliminate Miami from a major competition in Columbus, after knocking them out of the 2024 Leagues Cup thanks to a 3-2 victory at ScottsMiracle-Gro Field in the round of 16.

For a team boasting so much experience and talent in the biggest club and international competitions, Inter Miami failed to keep their composure in numerous instances last weekend.

Saturday’s defeat was the joint-worst for the club in the post-season, though given everything that transpired, they could be looking much worse heading into this match.

Kily Gonzalez will be relieved to know that Lionel Messi is available, escaping with just a caution in game one after throwing the ball at Gonzalo Tapia, a moment some believed should have warranted a sending off for the Argentine.

Miami played a good portion of that match a man down, and have never won the second game in a best-of-three MLS playoff series.

The Herons have given up a combined six goals in their last two matches versus MLS opposition and have yet to beat an MLS club in a knockout fixture this season.

The reigning league champions have lost four of their five all-time playoff outings away from home, with their only triumph coming at FC Cincinnati in 2025 (4-0).

Due to a cruciate ligament tear, Wessam Abou Ali will not be available for Columbus in game two of this series.

Gonzalo Tapia netted twice in Miami, with Taha Habroune also scoring as Patrick Schulte made three stops for the clean sheet.

On the Miami side, Mateo Silvetti is dealing with a shoulder issue, Tadeo Allende still has a knee problem and Israel Boatwright is recovering from a cruciate ligament tear.

Gonzalo Lujan will be suspended following his red card in game one, while Sergio Reguilon, David Ayala and Cesar Abadia are all questionable with lower-body injuries.

#2058. Five Man United players who could leave in January as part of major Mauricio Pochettino clear-out

By Matt Law | 25 Nov 2026 14:30, Last updated: 25 Nov 2026 14:32

Manchester United are expected to be active during the January transfer window, with the club potentially making three mid-season signings.

The Red Devils currently find themselves down in 19th spot in the Premier League table and facing what would be a stunning relegation from the top flight.

There could also be first-team departures in the winter market, as new head coach Mauricio Pochettino begins to shape the squad.

Here, Sports Mole looks at five players who could potentially leave Old Trafford when the January market opens for business.

Zirkzee was linked with a move away from Man United over the summer, but he was ultimately not allowed to depart due to the fact that the Red Devils were unable to sign a replacement.

Even with Benjamin Sesko out injured, Zirkzee has not started a single match this season, with each of his 11 appearances coming off the bench.

The Netherlands international has scored twice this season, but his overall record for Man United since arriving from Bologna in 2024 is 11 goals in 86 appearances.

It has been a struggle for the 25-year-old in English football, but he is not short of interest, and a return to Serie A in January is seen as a serious possibility at this stage.

Como and Napoli are the latest clubs to be linked with Zirkzee.

Mazraoui is reportedly attracting the attention of AC Milan ahead of the January market, with a mid-season departure for the Morocco international thought to be possible.

The 29-year-old has underperformed in his nine appearances for Man United this season and is not believed to be in the club's long-term plans.

Initially, the defender impressed after arriving from Bayern Munich, and he has now represented the Red Devils on 86 occasions in all competitions.

However, a switch in the January market could be on the cards, especially if Man United are able to boost their options at full-back in the winter window.

Heaven is reportedly open to the idea of leaving Man United during the winter market.

The 20-year-old has made eight appearances for the Red Devils this season, including five outings in the Premier League, but he was an unused substitute against Liverpool in the team's last match, which ended in a 4-0 defeat.

Man United are expected to bring in a new centre-back in January, and Heaven is said to be concerned over what that would mean for his playing time.

As a result, the Englishman is allegedly open to a winter loan move, while a permanent exit is not being ruled out depending on what offers arrive.

Heaven, who has a contract with Man United until the summer of 2029, has made 32 appearances for the club since arriving from Arsenal in February 2025.

Martinez's contract with Man United is due to expire at the end of the campaign, and there is no getting away from the fact that it has been a tough season for the Argentine.

A new deal for the 28-year-old is seen as highly unlikely given that he has openly criticised Man United chiefs, such as director of football Jason Wilcox, during the 2026-27 season.

Man United will be reluctant to lose the Argentina international on a free transfer, so it is possible that a sale in the January window could occur, depending on the offer.

Martinez has been one of the team's biggest under-performers this season, and it appears that his time at Old Trafford will come to an end at some stage in 2027.

Amass may yet be given the chance to prove himself to new Man United head coach Pochettino, but the defender's future looks to be away from Old Trafford.

The 19-year-old was heavily linked with a departure during the summer window, but he stayed at Man United past the deadline and has made two appearances this season, both in the Champions League.

Amass was not in the squad against Liverpool last time out, though, and Luke Shaw's return to fitness has pushed him further down the pecking order.

A number of Championship clubs are believed to be keen on the teenager, who only has a deal with the 20-time English champions until the end of the season.

#2057. No advantage played and an indirect free-kick: Pairetto criticised for Milan-Frosinone officiating

Referee Luca Pairetto has been criticised for his performance in AC Milan’s 5-1 win over Frosinone last night.

This morning’s edition of La Gazzetta dello Sport (seen below) comments on three incidents above all in which the referee ultimately seemed incapable of providing the right decision, one of which could have cost a goal.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

In the first half, Adrien Rabiot stole the ball from Cittadini, who blatantly dragged him back by the shirt. The ball rolled to Goncalo Ramos who was through on goal and had a man to his left, effectively making it a two-on-zero break with just the goalkeeper to beat.

Yet, the referee Pairetto blew his whistle for a foul and showed a yellow card to the defender. He immediately apologised for his error in failing to play the clear advantage for the Rossoneri, in an incident reminiscent of the Junior Messias goal not given against Spezia in 2021-22.

That wasn’t all, either. Nine minutes into the second half, an indirect free-kick was awarded inside the Frosinone penalty area following a clear back-pass from Calvani to Palmisani, that was actually flagged by the assistant referee and not initially given by Pairetto.

Former referee Mauro Bergonzi commented on the refereeing incidents in Milan-Frosinone to La Domenica Sportiva.

“In the first half, the referee failed to take the lead: he missed the lead, he squandered it, if we’re being honest. The situation is this: Cittadini fouls Rabiot, who steals the ball, a clear hold, the referee hastily calls the foul, and he also realises it immediately because he stops, as if to say, ‘Wow, it’s a shame I called’ because Gonçalo Ramos was hurtling toward goal.

“He apologizes, he says: ‘No, I apologise, I’m booking the Frosinone player.’ He said sorry. But this is something that really bothers you from a refereeing perspective because the lead was clear, but the referee’s gesture is commendable because he raises his hands and humbly says: ‘I made a mistake’.

“53rd minute: we rarely see them called on football pitches during matches. It’s a deliberate back pass from Calvani to the goalkeeper, the goalkeeper saves it, the referee wouldn’t have called anything, he turns to the right, looks at the assistant because the assistant signals it to him.

“Pairetto tells the assistant, ‘Well done’ and, to make matters worse, ‘Well done’ again. The assistant was good because he saw it perfectly correctly. He made his choice, he got it right because this is a pass, a deliberate back pass.

“Chiffi, the fourth official, is also there, pressing the button to communicate with the refereeing team via his headset, and if the assistant hadn’t seen it, the fourth official would have signaled it.”

#2056. Liverpool v Manchester United: Five classic wins at Anfield

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

From Diego Forlan’s unforgettable double to Juan Mata’s spectacular volley, Manchester United have enjoyed some memorable victories at Anfield over the years.

With Mauricio Pochettino preparing for his first match in charge against Liverpool on Sunday, we look back at five occasions when United emerged victorious from one of English football’s fiercest fixtures.

United travelled to Anfield on 19 April 1997 knowing Liverpool could overtake them at the top of the Premier League with a victory. Instead, centre-back Gary Pallister scored twice with headers from David Beckham deliveries, either side of John Barnes’s equaliser.

Andy Cole added a third after the interval, heading home from Gary Neville’s cross following an error by Liverpool goalkeeper David James. The victory helped Sir Alex Ferguson’s side take a decisive step towards retaining the Premier League title, which they secured the following month.

Fresh from winning the Treble, United returned to Anfield for a remarkable match in September 1999. Jamie Carragher headed into his own net inside three minutes before Andy Cole doubled United’s advantage.

Sami Hyypia pulled a goal back for Liverpool, but Carragher’s second own goal restored the visitors’ two-goal lead. Patrik Berger reduced the deficit again, and United faced a difficult finish after Cole was sent off for a second bookable offence. Despite playing the closing stages with 10 men, Ferguson’s side held on for a 3-2 victory.

Diego Forlan had endured a frustrating start to his United career before producing a performance that supporters would remember for years. The Uruguayan opened the scoring after Liverpool goalkeeper Jerzy Dudek failed to gather the ball, allowing him to finish from close range.

Forlan added a second just minutes later, beating Dudek at his near post to put United in control. Sami Hyypia pulled one back for Liverpool, but Ferguson’s side held on for a victory that proved valuable in their successful pursuit of the 2002-03 Premier League title. Forlan’s double also inspired a terrace song that remains familiar to United supporters.

Few United victories at Anfield have been as dramatic as the one secured by John O’Shea in March 2007. Liverpool had enjoyed the better opportunities, and United’s task became harder when Paul Scholes was sent off late in the second half.

With a goalless draw appearing increasingly likely, Cristiano Ronaldo’s free-kick was parried by goalkeeper Pepe Reina. Substitute O’Shea reacted quickest, firing the rebound high into the net in stoppage time. The unexpected winner gave United three valuable points on their way to reclaiming the Premier League title.

Juan Mata produced one of the most memorable individual performances in this fixture during Louis van Gaal’s first season as United manager. The Spaniard opened the scoring after 14 minutes, finishing a move involving Ander Herrera, before doubling United’s lead with a spectacular scissor-kick volley after half-time.

Liverpool captain Steven Gerrard had been sent off just 38 seconds after coming on as a substitute at the interval. Daniel Sturridge reduced the deficit, while Wayne Rooney missed a late penalty, but United held on for a 2-1 victory. Mata’s second goal remains one of the outstanding moments of United’s Premier League visits to Anfield.

Those five victories underline the different ways United have succeeded at Anfield, from outstanding individual performances to late winners and determined defensive displays. Pochettino now has an opportunity to begin his managerial reign with a memorable result against United’s biggest rivals.

Which United victory at Anfield is your favourite? Were you there for Forlan’s double, O’Shea’s winner or Mata’s volley? Share your memories in the comments below.

And it's fair to say the interim duo will not be short of backing against Steven Schumacher's side on Saturday week.

Rovers fans, of course, will not quite have the same feelings of animosity towards the Whites as they displayed against Burnley on November 22nd, but it is one of three eye-catching fixtures from their perspective - the other being against Preston North End.

Even if the journey is straightforward, those in the away end still had to part with a sizeable sum of cash, particularly if they are an adult-paying supporter.

Indeed, tickets in such brackets cost £36, whilst an adult wanting to take one junior supporter with them to the match still had to part with a combined £45 for the two tickets.

However, the aforementioned bundle was only available in the upper tier of the Toughsheet Community Stadium's away allocation.

This may come as little surprise too, as Bolton rank sixth in the Championship when it comes to the average price of a ticket for visiting supporters.

#2054. Manchester United’s 34-goal defensive record shows scale of Pochettino’s task

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Manchester United have conceded more goals than any other club in Europe’s five major domestic leagues this season, underlining the scale of the challenge facing Mauricio Pochettino following Sunday’s 4-0 defeat at Liverpool.

Pochettino’s first match in charge ended in a heavy defeat at Anfield after defender Leny Yoro was sent off in the eighth minute. Although the dismissal left United facing an uphill struggle, their defensive record suggests the new head coach has inherited problems that extend well beyond one match.

According to an analysis published by the Premier League, United have conceded 34 goals in 16 matches across all competitions this season. That is the highest total of any club in Europe’s five major domestic leagues, with Sunday’s defeat adding four more goals to an already concerning record.

The Premier League also calculated Liverpool’s expected goals at 4.38, suggesting the hosts created enough chances to have won by an even greater margin. United’s difficulties began when Matthijs de Ligt misjudged a long ball, and Yoro fouled Alexander Isak as the Liverpool forward attempted to break through on goal.

Dominik Szoboszlai scored from the resulting free-kick, with Florian Wirtz doubling Liverpool’s advantage just three minutes later. Rio Ngumoha and substitute Victor Munoz completed the scoring after half-time.

The defeat left United 19th in the Premier League, with just eight points from their opening 11 matches and one league victory all season. Pochettino acknowledged the painful nature of the result but argued that the early dismissal had transformed the contest. He also questioned whether Yoro should have been sent off, given the circumstances of the challenge on Isak.

The Argentine’s first tactical plan was disrupted almost immediately, making it difficult to draw firm conclusions about how United might perform under him with a full complement of players.

Nevertheless, the defensive record accumulated before his arrival makes clear that improving the team’s organisation will be a central part of his work. The Premier League’s analysis highlighted De Ligt’s mistake before the sending-off and the opportunities Liverpool continued to create against United’s depleted defence.

United return to Champions League action on Wednesday when they travel to Portugal to face Sporting, giving Pochettino little time to address the issues exposed at Anfield. The fixture offers an early opportunity to assess how he intends to organise his defence and whether the team can respond to another damaging result.

Pochettino must also decide how to balance defensive stability with the need to improve results. United’s league position means they cannot afford to spend too long finding that balance. The circumstances of Sunday’s defeat were unusual, and the red card undoubtedly made United’s task more difficult. But conceding 34 goals in 16 matches points to a wider problem that will require sustained improvement rather than a reaction to one result.

For Pochettino, making United harder to beat is an obvious starting point. Wednesday’s trip to Sporting will provide his next opportunity to begin that process.

By Joel Lefevre | 25 Nov 2026 14:36

Real Salt Lake will try to extend their season into next week with a Friday win over the Vancouver Whitecaps at America First Field in game two of that best-of-three playoff series.

The reigning MLS Cup runners-up captured game one 2-0 last Sunday, putting them one win away from round two in the Western Conference playoffs.

After surviving a nervy shootout in the Wild Card Round, Salt Lake are now on the brink of elimination yet again after a subpar showing in game one against the Caps.

On Sunday, Pablo Mastroeni’s men suffered just their second defeat in their last eight MLS outings and were second-best for much of the game.

While they are on the verge of another early exit, they can draw optimism from being unbeaten in their last four MLS affairs at America First Field.

During the regular season, Salt Lake showed strong attacking potency on home soil, netting three or more goals six times and scoring multiple times on 12 separate occasions.

They will try to end a five-match playoff losing run on Friday, scoring a goal or fewer in each of those instances.

Claret and Cobalt have not lost a playoff match at home in 90 minutes this decade, with their only such victory at America First Field occurring on penalties versus the Houston Dynamo in game two of that opening round series.

It is just one game, but Vancouver looked very much like title contenders as they began the 2026 MLS post-season in style.

Jesper Sorensen’s side carried the play throughout game one and will seek a fifth consecutive MLS triumph on Friday.

While this is the fourth straight year the Caps are in the post-season, they have won only once in that span in normal time away from home, beating San Diego 3-1 in the Western Conference Final last year.

Meanwhile, they failed to win three of their final four regular-season away fixtures this season against Western Conference opposition, with the highest-scoring team in the West failing to score on two of those occasions.

Vancouver have never lost a playoff encounter under Sorensen when netting first but have also never won a post-season outing in 90 minutes when conceding the opener under his leadership.

Sorensen, meanwhile, has won every match against RSL as the Caps boss, including their last two visits to America First Field, in which his team scored three goals both times.

Due to a hamstring strain, Ariath Piol may miss this match for RSL, while Diego Luna and Emeka Eneli are questionable because of knee injuries and Zach Booth still has to clear concussion protocol.

Sergi Solans has scored three goals in his last two MLS home outings, including the opener in their 2-1 win over the Los Angeles Galaxy on Decision Day, while Rafael Cabral kept game one close, making six saves, many of them spectacular.

On the Vancouver side, an illness may keep Belal Halbouni out on Friday, and it is unlikely we will see Kenji Cabrera feature as he deals with a foot injury.

Edier Ocampo got the Caps on the board late in the opening half of game one, with Bruno Caicedo adding some insurance in the final 45 minutes.

#2052. Roy Keane questions Manchester United standards after Liverpool humiliation

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Former Manchester United captain Roy Keane has criticised the players’ performance and questioned supporters applauding them after Sunday’s 4-0 defeat at Liverpool, insisting the early red card was no excuse for the manner of the loss.

Mauricio Pochettino’s first match in charge unravelled when Leny Yoro was sent off after eight minutes at Anfield. Liverpool scored twice inside 13 minutes and added two more goals after the break, leaving United 19th in the Premier League.

Speaking on Sky Sports, Keane challenged the suggestion that United’s performance was understandable because they had played most of the match with 10 men.

“Fans are applauding. Applauding what?” he asked, before describing the performance as “rubbish”.

Keane argued that United should have shown greater fight and quality, claiming Liverpool could have scored eight or nine goals. He also questioned why responsibility for the club’s problems so often fell on managers, owners and executives rather than the players themselves.

Some travelling supporters applauded the team after the final whistle, potentially recognising the difficulty of playing more than 80 minutes with a numerical disadvantage. Keane, however, viewed the reaction as evidence of declining expectations.

Keane also challenged fellow former United captain Gary Neville, who had suggested the early dismissal made the result more understandable. Neville was far from satisfied with United’s performance, though, and separately accused the club’s hierarchy of negligence for failing to strengthen the defence.

The disagreement reflected two concerns surrounding United: the players’ response to adversity and the quality of the squad available to Pochettino. Liverpool’s goals came from Dominik Szoboszlai, Florian Wirtz, Rio Ngumoha and Victor Munoz, with the hosts creating numerous further opportunities.

Pochettino defended his players’ commitment afterwards and questioned Yoro’s dismissal, arguing that Alexander Isak had not established control of the ball when the defender challenged him. United have taken just eight points from 11 Premier League matches, leaving their new manager with little time to improve results. They travel to Sporting in the Champions League on Wednesday before returning to domestic action.

Keane’s criticism adds to the scrutiny surrounding the squad following another heavy defeat to their fiercest rivals. For Pochettino, the immediate challenge is to improve defensive organisation while restoring confidence in a side struggling near the bottom of the table.

#2051. Tonda Eckert reveals Kim Hellberg phone 'conversation' ahead of Southampton v Middlesbrough

Southampton boss Tonda Eckert has confirmed he spoke to Middlesbrough boss Kim Hellberg in private ahead of the two clubs meeting for the first time since Spygate on Friday night.

As all football fans will remember from a story that dominated English football at the time, Saints were expelled from the play-offs after they admitted sending an employee to watch Boro train ahead of their play-off semi-final.

Therefore, despite losing to the south coast outfit over two legs, it was Middlesbrough who went to Wembley, although they did lose the final to Hull.

The punishment didn’t end there for Southampton, who admitted spying on several other Championship rivals earlier in the season, and they started the current campaign with a four-point deduction.

Given everything that had gone on, the first meeting between the two clubs was always going to be a spicy affair, and they face each other at St. Mary’s Stadium on Friday night, in a game that has been chosen for live TV coverage.

With Hellberg previously calling Southampton’s actions ‘disgraceful’, as well as stating what went on ‘breaks my heart’, the talking points ahead of the game were always going to extend beyond what happens on the pitch.

And, when asked about his relationship with Hellberg, Saints chief Eckert tried to play things down, as he revealed to journalist Alfie House that the pair did have discussions before the season started.

“We've had a conversation on the phone and we saw each other at the EFL day before the season. We are two managers who will try to win a game and that's it.”

You can understand Eckert’s comments here, and there’s no doubt that Hellberg will echo those words, as it’s all about trying to win the game.

Given everything that had gone on, the first time the two clubs met was always going to be a big deal though, and all eyes will be on the dugouts to see how the managers greet each other before kick-off.

But, both clubs are trying to move on from what happened, and, in some ways, they will just be glad to get this game out of the way, as a lot of the build-up and noise around the fixture is all based on last season.

FLW have ranked the 10 best players in the EFL Championship for the 2026/27 season aged 22 or below, including a pair of Middlesbrough stars.

Clearly, Middlesbrough were furious at what happened, but Saints will feel they were hit with a severe punishment, with the points deduction meaning they go into the game seven points behind Hellberg’s side, as opposed to three.

Ultimately, this is a huge game in the promotion race, and both clubs will know that they will be sending out a statement if they can emerge victorious.

Southampton have an exceptional home record this season, so they will fancy their chances, but a loss will leave a big gap to Boro in the battle for a top two finish.

Meanwhile, Middlesbrough know that Saints can apply real pressure to them with a win, and they risk dropping from first to fifth with a defeat on the south coast if results go against them this weekend.

Obviously, Spygate just adds to it all, but this is a game that will have massive implications on the promotion race - which Eckert and Hellberg will be well aware of.

#2050. Ramos upset by substitution: The clarification and why Cisse came on over Camarda

Goncalo Ramos got his name on the scoresheet in AC Milan’s rout of Frosinone, but he was forced off in the second half.

As Sky (via Calciomercato.com) report, everything seemed great when Milan were dominating Frosinone at San Siro and scoring nearly every time they went forward. However, during the second half, a minor alarm sounded.

Ruben Amorim was forced to replace his Ramos in the 50th minute. The Portuguese striker – who had scored in the first half – was hastily removed from the pitch by the head coach shortly after Ardon Jashari’s goal to make it 3-1.

The reason? The change was made due to a minor muscle problem that the player reported to the bench, even though Ramos himself did not seem happy about the substitution at the time of the change.

The former PSG man informed the bench that he had a small calf problem and – to avoid worsening the issue – Amorim immediately opted for a substitution. Alphadjo Cisse was sent on in his place, and he scored the fourth goal just a few minutes later.

When he was taken off, Ramos showed a grimace of disapproval towards his bench, miming the ‘wait’ gesture. He then complained that he would have liked to try to stay on the pitch, but some post-match comments cleared things up.

When asked if his calf is ok, he told DAZN: “Yes, yes, everything’s fine, everything’s fine.” Rabiot later revealed the background to the substitution to Sky, admitting that he played a hand in convincing the coach.

“Gonçalo is the first player to do a lot. Today I saw he had a bit of pain in his thigh. I know he’s a very important player for us, and we have a lot of matches. I even asked the coach to let him rest a bit,” the Frenchman said.

Speaking to DAZN after the final whistle, Amorim was asked why he felt it was right for Cisse to come on rather than Francesco Camarda, the only other natural centre-forward in the squad

“We didn’t spend many minutes near the penalty area; he was behind. Today we were 40 metres from the goal, and Cisse was perfect for that. That’s why I didn’t bring on Camarda for Ramos: I needed someone who could run in behind.”

#2049. Manchester United to appeal Leny Yoro red card as Fernandes questions officials

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Manchester United have notified the Football Association of their intention to appeal against Leny Yoro’s red card during Sunday’s 4-0 defeat at Liverpool, with captain Bruno Fernandes among those questioning the decision.

The French defender was dismissed in the eighth minute at Anfield after bringing down Alexander Isak outside the penalty area. Referee Sam Barrott ruled that Yoro had denied an obvious goalscoring opportunity, with Dominik Szoboszlai scoring from the resulting free-kick.

According to Sky Sports, United intend to challenge the decision, with Isak’s chances of regaining possession central to the dispute. Mauricio Pochettino questioned whether Isak would have reached the ball before goalkeeper Senne Lammens, a view shared by midfielder Kobbie Mainoo.

Fernandes told the BBC after the match that it was “never a red card”.

“To make a mistake like this can change a game,” he said, as reported by the Press Association. The Portuguese midfielder nevertheless accepted that United were responsible for conceding from the subsequent free-kick.

Former Premier League referee Dermot Gallagher offered a different assessment on Sky Sports’ Ref Watch programme, supporting Barrott’s decision while acknowledging the uncertainty over whether Isak would have reached the ball before Lammens.

“I think most people would have expected a red card,” Gallagher said.

An attacker’s likelihood of retaining or regaining possession is among the factors referees must consider when assessing whether a foul has denied an obvious goalscoring opportunity.

United host Brentford at Old Trafford on Saturday in Pochettino’s first home match in charge. Unless the appeal succeeds, Yoro will serve a one-match suspension against the Bees. United sit 19th in the Premier League, having won just one of their opening 11 matches. Yoro remains eligible for Wednesday’s Champions League fixture against Sporting, before attention turns to a crucial domestic fixture for Pochettino’s side.

#2045. "I don't know" - Steven Schumacher drops new Bolton Wanderers transfer claim

Bolton Wanderers boss Steven Schumacher simply doesn't know how big a budget he'll be able to work with in the January transfer window, despite knowing where in the squad he wants to strengthen.

The Trotters have had an okay start to life back in the Championship after a seven-year absence. Last season's League One play-off winners' main goal would have been to stay in the division, and thus far, they've remained above the dotted line.

Bolton sit 19th, with 18 points in 17 games. They've picked up some big wins, especially away from home, but are struggling at the Toughsheet Stadium and have only strung together back-to-back victories once.

At the moment, it's just one win in seven, with losses against out-of-form Bristol City and Derby County in that time, both of which saw them lead. It almost feels as if they're looking to get through the next month to assess, and then kick on in January.

However, with the winter window still a month away, Steven Schumacher is just focusing on picking up points between now and then.

Bolton haven't spent a weekend inside the relegation zone as yet, and will hope to put some more distance between them and the bottom three before January. But that hasn't stopped him from asking some early questions regarding recruitment come the new year.

"It’s in the very early stages from my point of view,” the Bolton boss said, per The Bolton News.

"The lads are obviously in the recruitment office, and they are working hard. Every time I see Jimmy Dickinson or Owen (Davies – lead recruitment and data analyst) or Fergal (Harkin, director of football), I'm asking if they've got any players for us, and the answer they keep saying is: ‘We'll tell you later!’

"So, we are focusing on the games for the time being, but that will start ramping up again in the next few weeks."

A look at the amount of pubs within a two-mile radius of every Championship ground this season.

Only Lincoln City are estimated to have a lower wage bill than Bolton this season, so it's a credit to Schumacher for keeping the Trotters competitive while still trying to play an entertaining, attacking brand of football.

There's still obvious room for improvement, though, and Schumacher knows where they need to strengthen. The question is, though, how much money will be available for them to do so?

"I have spoken to Fergal and Owen, and we are very clear about areas of the team that we would like to strengthen," he continued.

"Whether we can do or not, I don't know. But, yeah, we'll be positive that when we get to January, we'll be in a good place, because the recruitment lads work hard, and they'll do their best."

While Schumacher is remaining upbeat about the club's situation heading into January and the prospect of adding more quality, Bolton fans are a little more pessimistic regarding the fact that their head coach doesn't yet know how much will be available for him.

The Trotters endured a bit of a frustrating summer transfer window regarding player recruitment, especially in the forward areas.

Their pursuits of both Max Dean and Johnny Kenny fell flat, and while it allowed Sam Dalby to become an unexpected hero at the top end of the pitch, it didn't mask their annoyance at the lack of investment in the summer. Now, warning bells are ringing again.

"League 1 it is, then. It's obvious the club doesn't want to back the manager," one fan said in response to the Bolton News article on X. "Good manager being let down," another added, while a third claimed that Schumacher was being "set up to fail."

"It’s obvious a step up in the budget and therefore quality of recruitment is needed in January," was also among the replies, with frustrations being aired at the number of unproven, young arrivals at the club in the summer.

And one, quite plainly, wrote, "We don’t invest we’re goin down it’s as simple as that."

Bolton have scrapped well in the Championship this season, all things considered, but when the January window opens, and sides around them with more lucrative budgets improve their squad, Wanderers need to be wary of possibly being left behind.

Preston North End are already starting to make ground and can even climb out of the drop zone this weekend, and those below them, Burnley and Derby, should have the funds to try and drag themselves out of this hot water they're in.

If they get better, and Bolton continue operating at around a point per game, they may not have enough to avoid an immediate drop back to League One, which would be a massive shame, given the potential of the club under Schumacher and the crowds they can attract.

We'll see what the Trotters do during January, but it's already becoming apparent amongst some sections of the supporter base that their winter dealings could define their season.

#2043. What can Mauricio Pochettino spend on January targets at Manchester United?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United spent heavily during the summer, bringing Youri Tielemans, Andrey Santos and Carlos Baleba to Old Trafford for a combined £148million.

Despite the summer transfers, Michael Carrick failed to keep his job and was replaced by Mauricio Pochettino.

With United struggling, Pochettino is expected to bring a few new names in the January transfer window. The manager is reportedly expected to bring in no more than two players, with a new striker understood to be his biggest priority.

With the summer investment, the club is left with less flexibility as Pochettino looks to address several weaknesses in his squad.

Manchester United manager Mauricio Pochettino could have less than £50million to spend in January, leaving the Argentine with limited room to spend during the winter transfer window.

According to reports from the Daily Mail, United are working within financial restrictions and are unlikely to sanction any major spending in January. The club are also mindful of the Premier League’s financial regulations, which could make it difficult to fund major names.

Benjamin Sesko’s injury problems and Joshua Zirkzee’s disappointing form have increased the need for another centre-forward.

Monaco striker Folarin Balogun is among the names linked with United. Pochettino already knows the United States international from their time together with the national team, which could make him an interesting option.

Leeds United’s Dominic Calvert-Lewin and former Brentford striker Ivan Toney, currently playing for Al-Ahli, have also been mentioned as targets.

A left-back is another position United want to address. The club failed to bring in proper competition for Luke Shaw during the summer, despite being linked with Barcelona’s Alejandro Balde and Rayan Ait-Nouri.

However, the budget means Pochettino may have to choose his priorities carefully rather than address every problem in one window.

United recorded revenue of £677.6million despite not playing European football last season. However, the Premier League’s Squad Cost Ratio rules restrict spending on player and head coach wages, transfer repayments and agent fees to 85 per cent of football revenue, plus net profit from player sales.

January could become a window of careful decisions for United and Pochettino but looking at their current position and form, they need far more than just 50 million. In this budget, Pochettino needs to be careful who he brings in to improve their standings.

#2042. Too late, FA overturns Leny Yoro’s Liverpool red card in Pochettino’s first game

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have received a major boost ahead of their Premier League meeting with Brentford after the Football Association overturned Leny Yoro’s red card against Liverpool.

The defender was sent off just eight minutes into Sunday’s 4-0 defeat at Anfield after bringing down Alexander Isak during a challenge that referee Sam Barrott judged to be a denial of a clear goalscoring opportunity.

However, Manchester United decided to contest the decision, and their appeal has now been successful. The FA confirmed that Yoro’s one-match suspension has been removed following a successful claim of wrongful dismissal.

“The Manchester United player was sent off for denying an obvious goalscoring opportunity during their Premier League match against Liverpool on Sunday 22 November. The independent Regulatory Commission’s written reasons for its decision will be published in due course,” the FA’s official statement read.

The decision means the French defender is available for Saturday’s meeting with Brentford at Old Trafford, giving Mauricio Pochettino another option in defence.

Mauricio Pochettino was furious with the original decision, particularly because it came so early in the game and left United playing with ten men for almost the entire match.

The United manager believed Isak’s heavy touch had taken the ball away from the Liverpool forward and put Senne Lammens in a position to collect it.

Speaking after the defeat, Pochettino questioned whether Yoro had actually committed a foul and insisted the defender had no intention of bringing Isak down.

“The ball wasn’t in his control,” Pochettino said.

“Isak, when he saw the control wasn’t good, it’s normal to delay, and stop to the run. In that moment that Yoro came from the back, and you go down.”

The Argentine also made his feelings clear about the nature of the contact.

“But all that we play football; you know that wasn’t intentional. That is never a foul,” he added.

The FA’s decision will come as a relief to Manchester United but perhaps too late. United suffered a heavy 4-0 defeat at Anfield after Yoro’s red. Pochettino would have had a chance in the game if VAR had overturned the red card, but United got another decision against them in the Premier League.

Although the appeal cannot change the outcome of the heavy defeat at Anfield, it at least means Pochettino will not lose one of his defensive options because of a decision that the club successfully challenged.

#2041. Maritimo vs Nacional - prediction, team news, lineups

By Adeyeye Oluwapelumi | 25 Nov 2026 16:48

Maritimo and Nacional renew their Madeira derby rivalry in the Primeira Liga on Friday evening, with both sides looking to claim an important victory and move further clear of the drop zone.

The two clubs are meeting in the top flight for the first time in five years, with the visitors sitting 13th in the Primeira Liga table on 10 points from 11 matches, just two points above the 15th-placed hosts, who have a game in hand against Estoril Praia and sit outside the relegation playoff place on head-to-head points ahead of Casa Pia.

Maritimo made an encouraging start to life back in the top flight, winning their opening two league fixtures against Casa Pia and Famalicao, but they have not claimed another Primeira Liga victory since beating the latter on August 16.

Mitchell van der Gaag's side have since gone eight league matches without a win (D2, L6), including defeats in their last three such outings against Porto (2-1), Vitoria de Guimaraes (5-0) and Santa Clara (2-0).

Only bottom-placed Estoril, who are yet to win a league match this season, have endured a longer winless run, while Maritimo have scored the third-fewest goals in the division (nine) and conceded the third-most (21).

Van der Gaag's men have also struggled to make home advantage count, recording just one victory from five league matches at their own ground and losing the last three, during which they conceded multiple goals on each occasion and scored just once.

Maritimo did, however, receive a welcome boost in the Taca de Portugal last weekend, progressing past Chaves following a goalless draw and a 3-2 penalty shootout victory, and will hope that result provides some momentum ahead of Friday's derby.

Nacional have also endured a difficult start to the campaign, but they have collected six points from their last three matches, including a 1-0 victory over Academico de Viseu courtesy of Matchoi Djalo's late winner.

That result marked the first victory under new boss Joao Henriques, who arrived in late October as the club's third permanent manager of the season following the departures of Joao Giao and Alexandre Santos.

Henriques initially struggled to build on interim coach Bruno Abreu's home victory over Braga, losing away to Arouca in his first match in charge, but the latest success has given the Alvinegros an opportunity to record consecutive competitive wins for the first time since beating Alverca and Tondela in April.

Interestingly, Nacional's last four matches have each produced just one goal, including their disappointing Taca de Portugal defeat to lower-division Sanjoanense on October 17, meaning taking maximum points on Friday could hinge on greater efficiency in attack and defensive discipline.

However, the Islanders still have plenty of room for improvement away from home, having collected just one point from five Primeira Liga trips this season, drawing at Santa Clara on the opening weekend before losing their next four against Vitoria, Sporting, Rio Ave and Arouca.

Nacional have also found this fixture difficult in recent meetings, losing four of their last five league encounters with Maritimo across the Primeira Liga and Liga 2 (D1), including a 3-1 defeat on their most recent competitive visit.

Maritimo are monitoring defenders Rafael Fernandes (muscle) and Paulo Henrique (thigh), who both missed last weekend's cup tie against Chaves.

Yellu Santiago is also working his way back to full fitness from an injury, having only featured in the opening weekend of the Primeira Liga campaign.

Goncal Tabuaco's performance against Chaves has given Van der Gaag a decision to make in goal after he replaced Fran Vieites in the cup, saving two penalties during the shootout.

Nacional will be without Ulisses Wilson and Filipe Soares, who are both recovering from ankle injuries and remain unavailable.

Leo Santos (knee) is also sidelined, while Brazilian duo Wesley Gasolina and Liziero continue to recover from their respective injuries.

Henriques could retain the lineup that started against Viseu, with Miguel Baeza anchoring the midfield, although Djalo is pushing for a starting place after making an impact from the bench last time out.

#2040. West Brom coach eyed up for Lincoln City manager role

Lincoln City are on the hunt for a third managerial figure this calendar year following the midweek decision to part ways with joint-managers, Chris Cohen and Tom Shaw.

The duo led the Imps into their first Championship season since 1961 following Michael Skubala's summer exit to divisional rivals, Bristol City.

Lincoln coasted to last season's League One title, accumulating a staggering 103 points whilst also embarking on a 29-match unbeaten run.

But as the final month of 2026 nears, they are the lowest-placed out of the three sides who earned promotion last term, having taken just 15 points from their first 17 matches.

It remains to be seen which direction is taken by owner, Ron Fowler at the ProAmpac Stadium, but it has emerged that a previous target, now working with another Championship club, has entered the managerial frame.

Lincoln are currently outside the relegation zone by three points, with Tuesday's heavy 4-0 defeat to Charlton Athletic at The Valley, coupled with the manner of their side's performance, spelt the end for Cohen and Shaw.

The Imps have won just one of their last six Championship matches, with it now emerging through Alan Nixon - via Patreon - that Gavin Strachan has been listed as a potential candidate.

Strachan, 47, is currently on the coaching staff at West Bromwich Albion under James Morrison, with the Baggies currently within three points of the Championship summit after a 3-1 win at Bolton Wanderers.

Prior to the start of the new season, the Aberdeen-born man spent six years at Celtic working under the likes of Brendan Rodgers, Ange Postecoglou and Martin O'Neill, with the Hoops winning the last five Scottish Premiership titles on offer.

Following on from Postecoglou's exit to Tottenham Hotspur three years ago, the Australian wished to take Strachan with him to North London, but a deal could not be agreed.

The aforementioned Morrison was appointed as permanent head coach of West Brom this summer after ensuring the Black Country side's survival in difficult circumstances.

As well as retaining Matt Gill, who had been linked with a switch to Leicester City, the former Scotland international midfielder opted to install Strachan and Tony McMahon as part of a new-look coaching setup.

Given how highly Strachan is rated on the coaching circuit, Morrison's initial words following the decision read: "I’m also delighted to add Gavin Strachan to the group.

"Having a new pair of eyes from somebody with Gavin’s experience will help us massively. He has worked alongside some top managers, like Martin O’Neill, Ange Postecoglou and Brendan Rodgers, and will no doubt bring fresh ideas with him."

Prior to his spell with Celtic, Strachan also assisted Darren Ferguson in two spells at Peterborough United, as well as his stint with Doncaster Rovers.

Football League World considers five potential managers Lincoln City could eye after sacking Chris Cohen and Tom Shaw

The decision for the Imps to remove Cohen and Shaw from their positions as joint-managers was the sixth managerial change already made in this season's Championship.

Four of those have been made by clubs in a similar region of the table to Lincoln, with Watford, Burnley and Preston North End all replacing Alessio Dionisi, Nicky Hayen and Paul Heckingbottom with Edward Iordanescu, Fabio Celestini and Graham Alexander respectively.

Lincoln fans, would Gavin Strachan be a wise appointment at this particular moment? Comment below!

With the Reds needing to rediscover form, particularly on home turf, in a bid to survive against the odds, supporters will be hoping that the board get their next decision right.

However, it has already been confirmed that no final call will be made ahead of Saturday's daunting task of taking on Wolves, with Shaun Pearson taking interim charge.

Manchester United could be facing another unwanted academy departure, with Ayden Heaven open to leaving Old Trafford on loan when the January transfer window opens.

The 20-year-old defender is concerned about his lack of regular football under Mauricio Pochettino and believes a temporary move could be the best option for his development, as reported by the BBC.

Ayden Heaven was an unused substitute during United’s 4-0 defeat against Liverpool on Sunday as Manchester United battle near the bottom of the Premier League table.

The England Under-20 international has already worked under three different managers since joining United from Arsenal in February 2025 in a much hyped move. He enjoyed a period of regular football under Ruben Amorim but lost his place under Michael Carrick, making occasional appearances.

🚨Exclusive: Manchester United's Ayden Heaven is open to a January move. The defender, 20, believes club's ongoing struggles may limit his chances but is keen to take the next step in his development. Loan preferred but permanent not ruled out.https://t.co/xTmbjoKo1W

#2037. Frosinone boss Alvini content with aggressive approach vs. Milan despite ‘naive’ mistakes

Frosinone head coach Massimiliano Alvini has admitted that his side simply gave AC Milan too many opportunities.

It has been a very positive return to the top flight for Frosinone so far, given that a point last night at San Siro would have seen them move back into the European places. However, they were ultimately given a bit of a humbling by Milan, who won 5-1 thanks to a ruthless counter-attacking display.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Some have questioned if the Canarini were a bit too aggressive in their approach to the game, given they played with such a high line and allowed space for Adrien Rabiot, Diego Moreira, Goncalo Ramos, Ardon Jashari, Alphadjo Cisse and Christian Pulisic to exploit.

Alvini was present in the press conference room at San Siro after the full-time whistle and he analysed what was perhaps the most difficult night of the season for his team. MilanNews transcribed his assessments.

Should the game have been played differently?

“Frosinone played their game but made a few mistakes they don’t usually make, but that’s certainly not my concern, losing by such a large margin. What’s worrying are the mistakes we made that we could have avoided.”

“We made a mistake on the first goal, which was a mistake of our own, while the other goals we conceded were goals we absolutely could have avoided. We conceded them and were naive.

“It’s a mistake of our own, and so today, from this perspective, if you come to Milan against Milan and make these mistakes, it’s only natural that we end up losing like this, by such a large scoreline.

“That’s the analysis, even though I think the scoreline is even too large: the first half, in my opinion, was extraordinary on our part.”

Did the absences have an impact, forcing you to experiment a bit?

“There’s nothing experimental about it. We played with the same principles, running forward to get to Rabiot. We preferred to bring on a defender who would push forward rather than a midfielder.

“In my opinion, this definitely didn’t influence the result, but rather the attitude, the idea, or the match strategy. And we certainly made – as I said before and I’ll repeat – mistakes we don’t usually make.

“If you give Milan certain chances, with such strong players and the quality of this team, you risk losing, and that’s what happened. You risk losing with a heavy defeat, and that’s what happened.

“He had a problem with that back heel in the box, and he definitely needs tests. It’s too early now; we’ll have to wait for the tests, which will be done starting Tuesday, but it’s definitely a problem.”

“We had a chance with Monterisi after a minute, we hit the post, and we created a lot. I have nothing to say to the team for their attitude and the way they played.

“We, I repeat, made some naivety, some mistakes. It’s also up to me to understand that there are important players on the other side who can cause problems.”

“It’s a result that definitely needs to be managed, navigated, and channeled, but it doesn’t change my attitude at all. We have 20 points today. The path we need to take is to try to stay in Serie A, and we’ll try to do so with this attitude, with this identity: this is the path we’ll take.

“Four years ago, I drew a game against Milan by playing defensively, and we took home a point. Today we made some mistakes, but this is the path we’ll take. I’m happier with the management, with the team’s identity, to try to achieve that objective, and this is what we’ll continue to do.”

#2036. ‘Not Manchester United standard’, Paul Merson raises concerns over Manchester United squad

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have failed to hit the numbers expected off them after a lackluster campaign so far.

Michael Carrick was relieved off his duties after winning just 1 game in his first 10, while collecting only 8 points. Manchester United arr currently languishing in the relegation spots and Carrick’s successor, Mauricio Pochettino, has a massive task ahead.

Manchester United have been leaky in defence and haven’t gone a game without conceding a goal this season. Their attacking returns are equally disappointing with United failing to convert chances regularly.

Paul Merson believes Manchester United must strengthen their defence in January but insists their problems extend beyond the backline, with concerns over their midfield and attacking options also needing attention.

The January transfer window could be the first stop for Pochettino for some shopping but the budget given to him as per a previous report by readmanutd isn’t much to make a difference.

Paul Merson believes the club have several areas to improve rather than focusing only on their defence.

Speaking on Sky Sports, the former Arsenal midfielder made it clear that United need to bring in defensive reinforcements.

“Manchester United will have to get centre-halves in, defenders in and full-backs in,” Merson said.

However, Merson does not believe defensive recruitment alone will solve United’s problems. He also questioned the team’s ability to control games in midfield and expressed concerns about the quality of their attacking options.

“I wouldn’t say that’s just been the problem. I don’t think they’ve got legs in midfield, I don’t think they’re dominating games in midfield,” he explained.

He was also critical of United’s forward line, suggesting that the current attacking options are not consistently performing.

“I’ve never really been a massive fan of their front three. I think they’re not Manchester United standard, in my opinion.”

Merson also compared the current generation with some of the club’s former greats, including Wayne Rooney, Ruud van Nistelrooy, Roy Keane, Paul Scholes, David Beckham and Cristiano Ronaldo. He compared players’ consistency with those earlier.

“Back in the day when it’s Rooney and them kind of players and Van Nistelrooy and Keane and Scholes and Beckham and Ronaldo, they were seven, eight out of ten every week. There wasn’t a four out of ten and a nine out of ten.”

“And if they have a four the week after, it doesn’t really matter. So for me, it hasn’t just been the defence.”

United could face difficult decisions when the transfer window opens, particularly if they want to strengthen multiple areas of the squad and with the budget available to Pochettino, that doesn’t seem to be the case.

#2034. Champions League contenders PSG and Bayern Munich face contrasting attacking crises ahead of crucial tests

By Naim Beneddra | 25 Nov 2026 17:05

Paris Saint-Germain and Bayern Munich enter Wednesday's Champions League fixtures facing significant questions about their attacking strength, with injuries forcing Luis Enrique and Vincent Kompany to reconsider how their teams operate.

The reigning European champions host Roma without Desire Doue and Khvicha Kvaratskhelia, two of the players most responsible for providing individual creativity in Luis Enrique's fluid attacking system. Bayern, meanwhile, travel to Lille without Harry Kane, whose influence extends well beyond his remarkable goalscoring record.

Both clubs remain among the contenders for the European title, but the nature of their problems differs considerably. PSG must compensate for losing two players capable of creating advantages from wide positions, while Bayern are missing the central figure around whom much of their attacking play has been organised.

Their circumstances are also different in the league phase. Paris have won all four matches and collected 12 points, while Bayern sit 13th with eight points following two victories and two draws. Wednesday's matches will provide the first European examination of how both sides respond to their recent setbacks.

PSG's problems began on October 20, when Kvaratskhelia suffered an anterior cruciate ligament injury during the 4-2 victory over Barcelona. The Georgian subsequently underwent surgery, leaving Luis Enrique without one of his most effective players in one-on-one situations.

The situation worsened on Saturday when Doue sustained a similar injury during the 3-2 Ligue 1 win at Nice. The France international had scored earlier in the match but was forced off before half time and now faces an extended recovery period.

The absence of both players removes two important sources of creativity from an attack built around movement, positional exchanges and the ability to beat defenders individually.

Kvaratskhelia's influence is particularly apparent on the left, where his dribbling and ability to move inside create space for overlapping teammates. Doue offers a different range of solutions, operating from the right while frequently drifting into central positions to combine with midfielders or attack the penalty area.

Losing both simultaneously could make PSG's attacking patterns easier for opponents to anticipate, particularly when facing teams prepared to defend in a compact shape.

Luis Enrique still has considerable resources. Ousmane Dembele is available after returning from a hamstring injury, while Maghnes Akliouche, Ferran Torres and Mika Godts provide options across the attacking line. However, finding an effective combination is likely to require more than simply replacing the two injured players with others occupying the same positions.

The Spaniard has nevertheless rejected suggestions that PSG must respond immediately in the transfer market.

"We don't need to sign players in the transfer window," Luis Enrique said ahead of the Roma encounter. "It's not our style to sign a player for just six months. We're happy with our squad."

He also emphasised the resilience his team have demonstrated in recent seasons, insisting that the injuries would not diminish their ambitions.

Wednesday's meeting with Gian Piero Gasperini's Roma presents a particularly demanding test. The Italian coach favours aggressive defending and a flexible tactical approach, potentially challenging PSG's ability to progress through central areas without relying so heavily on their absent wingers.

Gasperini acknowledged the significance of the injuries but warned against underestimating Paris.

"PSG are a proper team and can also make up for the absences of very good players with their own organisation," he said during his pre-match press conference.

For Luis Enrique, maintaining that collective identity while introducing new attacking combinations will be essential to PSG's hopes of challenging for a third successive European crown.

Bayern's immediate problem is concentrated through the middle of their attack, where Kane's absence has removed the team's most influential forward.

The England captain suffered a syndesmosis ligament injury during Friday's 1-1 Bundesliga draw with Cologne and subsequently underwent surgery. He is expected to miss several months, depriving Kompany of a player who had already contributed 10 goals and 10 assists in 16 appearances this season.

Those figures illustrate the difficulty of replacing Kane. His value lies in his ability to drop into midfield, connect attacks and create space for teammates, as well as his finishing inside the penalty area.

A conventional replacement might occupy the same central position without providing the passing range and movement that allow Bayern's wide players to attack the spaces Kane creates.

Kompany's options have been further reduced by the absence of Ismael Saibari, who is recovering from an ankle injury, and Serge Gnabry. Michael Olise has also been ruled out of the Lille fixture with a foot problem, removing another significant source of creativity and attacking production.

There is some encouragement in the return of Luis Diaz, who is available again after a minor hamstring injury, while Jamal Musiala is working his way back towards full fitness. Neither development, however, eliminates the challenge of reorganising an attack that has relied heavily on Kane's contribution.

Bayern sporting director Max Eberl acknowledged that the team would need to adapt its approach.

"The dynamic of our play must change to an extent," Eberl told the club's official website. "We have to try with the team to defend well over the whole game and score the required goals."

Kompany has similarly focused on the players available rather than those missing, describing the situation as exceptional but insisting that Bayern retain enough quality to compete.

"It's a chance to get wins in a different way," the Belgian said. "Now we have to show energy and mentality."

Unlike PSG, Bayern have less room for error in the league-phase standings. Their eight points leave them outside the top eight places that guarantee direct qualification for the last 16, making the visit to Lille important beyond its tactical implications.

The two Champions League contenders therefore approach Wednesday with different priorities. PSG must preserve the variety of their wide attacking play, while Bayern need to establish a new way of creating and finishing chances without Kane.

The performances against Roma and Lille will offer an early indication of which adjustments are effective, although neither match alone can establish how the injuries will shape the remainder of their European campaigns.

#2033. New Swansea City manager search update emerges ahead of Cardiff

The first South Wales derby between Swansea City and Cardiff City for just short of two years takes place on Sunday, and clarity has been provided regarding the hosts' search for a new head coach.

Swansea started the Championship campaign in fine fettle, leading the division as recently as October 17th under Vitor Matos, who then made the decision to join RC Strasbourg, much to the bemusement of those in South Wales.

The Portuguese was only installed as Alan Sheehan's replacement 12 months ago, winning 22 of his 47 matches in charge, with the last of those being the late loss at Birmingham City on November 7th.

Within a rather frosty announcement by the Welsh side, it was confirmed that Kris O'Leary, Ashley Williams, Joe Allen and Leon Britton were to lead the 2-2 draw against West Bromwich Albion last weekend, before also overseeing Tuesday's 2-1 loss to league leaders, Middlesbrough.

With ownership duo, Brett Cravatt and Jason Cohen, seeking a swift replacement, clarity has now been provided as to who will lead the Swans for the weekend's grudge match against the Bluebirds.

Despite a recent dip in form, Swansea are narrowly outside the early-season play-off places, and, if results fall their way in the next 72 hours, they could return to the top eight.

The club's current situation has led to intense speculation over who could take the reins, with Rob Edwards and Tommy Elphick initially linked, before reports emerged over the potential hire of Rosenborg boss, Freyr Alexandersson.

Alexandersson's current employers are currently fourth in the Norwegian Eliteserien and have three matches to play in a bid to secure European qualification.

Despite the 44-year-old acknowledging such links, it has been revealed by Sky Sports' Anthony Joseph that the quartet of Williams, O'Leary, Britton and Allen will take charge against Cardiff, and, potentially, the three subsequent fixtures as Alexandersson may see out the remainder of the campaign at the Lerkendal Stadion.

Unsurprisingly, the fact Swansea may now have to wait at least two more weeks for full clarity on who takes the long-term reins in the dugout has sparked an immediate reaction.

One of many responses read: "Whilst the derby was always going to be a bit of a push. We can't wait until 13th, that's another 4 games and a significant amount of points on the table and then to ask him to settle over christmas period. It has to be now or never for me."

Another wrote: "If we have to wait until 13th December, move on."

"Will be like when we persisted with Sheehan last season, need a new manager in asap," said another Swans fan.

"Potentially another 4 games in charge before we hire a new manager is bonkers," backed up the general feeling amongst the fanbase.

Another fan gave an in-depth stance on the matter, replying: "Waiting until mid-December to make an appointment is potentially catastrophic for our season, especially when waiting for a manager with a completely unproven track record. December is the busiest stretch of the season, it’s not a month that you can afford to throw away."

Swans fans, how are you feeling about this update ahead of the South Wales derby? Comment below!

However, some took a more philosophical outlook, with one comment reading: "The right appointment is the much better option than the quick appointment. Fans moaned on here the other day when it seemed he'd walk out on Rosenborg as he would be "another Matos" to us and leave us quickly. Now they moan he isn't leaving them in the lurch. You have to laugh."

Whilst uncertainty on the managerial front could be a hindrance for Swansea, especially with games against Bristol City, Bolton Wanderers and Charlton Athletic on the horizon potentially before a decision is made, in the short-term, the presence of Williams and Co. on the touchline may be a major benefit against Cardiff.

With the quartet boasting over 1,200 appearances for the club, they are aware, more than most, of how much this derby means to the locals, especially having lost the last meeting to a Bluebirds side who were ultimately relegated in rock-bottom to League One.

It also remains to be seen whether an upturn in form lands the Wales assistant a chance of the permanent role, but that will not be on Williams' mind right now.

#2032. Manchester United handed Luke Shaw injury boost ahead of Sporting clash

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Luke Shaw has returned to the Manchester United squad ahead of tonight’s Champions League fixture against Sporting in Lisbon.

The England international has missed the last eight matches in all competitions through injury. Shaw has not played since the 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur at Old Trafford on 10 October. The 31-year-old trained with his team-mates at Carrington on Tuesday before being included in Mauricio Pochettino’s 22-man travelling squad.

Pochettino worked with Shaw during the early stages of the defender’s career at Southampton. Speaking at Tuesday’s press conference, the United head coach was reluctant to say whether the left-back would feature against Sporting.

“Obviously, it’s good to see him and he came from a small issue but now he needs to recover his best form,” Pochettino told the club’s official website. Lisandro Martinez started at left-back against Liverpool on Sunday before moving into central defence following Leny Yoro’s dismissal.

Goalkeeper Karl Darlow is also back in the squad, although Marcus Rashford, Carlos Baleba, Manuel Ugarte and Benjamin Sesko remain unavailable. Matheus Cunha is suspended following his red card against Roma.

United have six points from their first four Champions League fixtures, having beaten Sabah and Roma at Old Trafford. Wednesday’s match will be Pochettino’s first European fixture in charge of the club.

With Brentford visiting Old Trafford at the weekend, Pochettino has two opportunities this week to assess Shaw’s match fitness, should the defender be ready to play.
