#2031. Chelsea World Cup Knockout Load Gives Xabi Alonso Pre-Season Problem

Chelsea’s World Cup contingent has moved from a group-stage watchlist into a genuine Xabi Alonso planning problem.

Chelsea’s own tournament update confirms that Enzo Fernandez, Mike Penders, Moises Caicedo, Kendry Paez, Malo Gusto and Jorrel Hato have reached the Round of 32.

England have also progressed after their final Group L win over Panama, which keeps Reece James and Trevoh Chalobah inside the wider knockout picture.

For supporters, that is a status marker. For Alonso, it is where summer pride starts to become pre-season compression.

The concern is not simply volume. It is where the minutes are landing.

Enzo has not missed a minute for Argentina, while Caicedo has remained central for Ecuador and captained them in the 2-1 win over Germany.

Those are not fringe workloads. They are the two midfield profiles Chelsea have built large parts of their control game around.

Alonso’s first Chelsea pre-season was always going to demand clarity through the middle of the pitch.

Instead, the players most central to that reset are still operating in knockout football.

Caicedo’s Ecuador role is the cleanest example. Read Chelsea has already assessed how his captaincy display against Germany strengthened his leadership case, but the next layer is more complicated.

Every additional knockout tie gives him more tournament rhythm, more authority and more fatigue.

Argentina’s route against Cape Verde keeps him on a glamorous stage at exactly the moment Chelsea need to settle his role, mood and tactical responsibilities under Alonso.

If Madrid-related noise continues around him, the club cannot afford a sluggish July handover.

Reece James started England’s first two matches before a hamstring issue ruled him out of the Panama game, according to the Guardian.

England still topped the group and moved into a last-32 tie with DR Congo, but James’ situation is exactly the kind of detail Chelsea’s medical team will monitor closely.

Chelsea have already had to plan around a large World Cup group.

The broader issue was clear in Read Chelsea’s earlier pre-season workload piece, but the knockout stage sharpens it because the returns will now be staggered.

Enzo brings a maximum group-stage load. Caicedo brings captaincy responsibility and heavy Ecuador minutes.

James brings knockout qualification mixed with another hamstring-management question.

Gusto and Hato remain tied to France and the Netherlands, while Penders and Paez are still absorbing tournament environments before club reintegration.

That is a spine issue, not a fringe issue.

Alonso needs automatisms in possession, spacing around the double pivot, clearer full-back behaviour and pressing triggers from midfield.

Those details do not bed in properly when key players arrive in drips after a major summer tournament.

Players returning from knockout football can bring competitive sharpness that closed-door friendlies cannot replicate.

Caicedo captaining Ecuador through pressure games matters. Enzo playing high-leverage minutes for Argentina matters.

Gusto and Hato absorbing tournament detail also helps Chelsea’s defensive reset.

But Chelsea cannot treat every tournament minute as clean preparation.

Alonso must decide who returns straight into tactical work, who needs a genuine pause and who can handle August without creating an avoidable injury bill by September.

That is where the James situation feels central.

If Chelsea’s captain carries a World Cup hamstring warning, the club’s right-sided structure becomes more than a selection debate.

It becomes an availability question that touches Gusto, the wider defensive rebuild and the need for control from midfield.

Read Chelsea has already looked at James’ captaincy test under Alonso, and the injury layer now makes that test sharper.

This is the hidden cost of a strong international footprint.

Chelsea want players good enough to survive deep into tournaments.

Alonso now has to make sure that status does not leave his first league weeks short of the very control those players are meant to provide.

#2030. Pochettino defends Manchester United mentality ahead of Sporting clash

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Mauricio Pochettino has defended the mentality of his Manchester United players despite their poor start to the season.

The Argentine suffered a 4-0 defeat at Liverpool on Sunday in his first match since replacing Michael Carrick. United sit 19th in the Premier League after 11 matches, with only Crystal Palace below them. However, speaking ahead of Wednesday’s Champions League fixture against Sporting in Lisbon, Pochettino rejected suggestions he had inherited a fragile group of players.

“The situation is fragile,” Pochettino told reporters, as quoted by the Press Association. “We are not fragile. We are a strong team.”

The United head coach pointed to last season’s third-place finish under Carrick as evidence of the squad’s capabilities. He also spoke of the commitment shown by his players in the dressing room at Anfield despite the manner of Sunday’s defeat.

United played much of that match with 10 men following Leny Yoro’s eighth-minute dismissal, a decision that has since been overturned on appeal. Wednesday’s fixture will give Pochettino another opportunity to assess his players.

Unlike their Premier League campaign, United’s European results have been mixed rather than consistently poor. They have won two and lost two of their opening four Champions League fixtures, while Sporting have collected nine points.

Pochettino insisted his immediate priority was the match in Lisbon rather than Saturday’s visit of Brentford to Old Trafford.

#2029. Malo Gusto’s Kante Lesson Gives Chelsea A £75m Xabi Alonso Decision

Malo Gusto’s World Cup has suddenly become more than a France subplot for Chelsea. It is now a live assessment of whether Xabi Alonso inherits a right-back worth protecting, reshaping or trading at the peak of his market.

Chelsea’s own interview with Gusto this week underlined the human part of that equation. The 23-year-old spoke about learning from N’Golo Kante, calling the former Blue one of the best midfielders France have produced and stressing the value of his example on and off the pitch.

That matters because Kante is not just a nostalgic reference point. He remains the standard Chelsea supporters still use when judging humility, intensity and reliability in a high-pressure dressing room.

For Gusto, those qualities arrive at a decisive moment. Reports around the player’s future claim Chelsea value him at around £75million, with the imminent Marco Palestra deal creating a more crowded full-back picture.

That number looks aggressive, but the logic is clear. Gusto is young, capped by France, under contract, Premier League-tested and capable of playing in several defensive structures.

🚨🚨| NEW: Chelsea are open to selling Malo Gusto this summer, with his agent already in 𝐓𝐀𝐋𝐊𝐒 with Manchester City and Inter. Chelsea will only sell if their valuation is met.{@siphillipssport} pic.twitter.com/vNR2bWud9F

The easiest reading of Gusto’s Kante comments is sentimental. It should not be.

For Alonso, who officially begins work on July 1, the important detail is what those comments say about the player Chelsea have been developing.

Gusto has not always looked completely settled at Stamford Bridge. His best games have carried thrust, speed and recovery power, while his less convincing spells have raised questions about final-ball quality and defensive concentration.

That uncertainty is exactly why the next coach matters.

Alonso’s previous teams have leaned heavily on wide players who can receive under pressure, attack space early and still protect the back line when possession breaks down. Gusto has the athletic base for that job.

The test is whether his decision-making can be refined enough to make him a structural piece rather than a rotation asset.

That is where the Kante reference lands with force. Chelsea do not need Gusto to imitate Kante’s position. They need him to absorb the same professional standard: repeat actions, clean defensive choices, no wasted emotion and total commitment to the collective rhythm.

The Marco Palestra deal changes the right side of Chelsea’s squad immediately. Read Chelsea has already examined how Palestra’s arrival offers a tactical clue over Alonso’s defensive plans, and that pressure is now sharpened by market noise around Gusto.

Chelsea can frame the situation in two ways.

One is profit-led. If a club comes close to the reported £75million level, selling a player signed from Lyon for a much lower fee becomes a powerful PSR lever.

The other is football-led. If Alonso sees Gusto as a high-upside wing-back who fits his model, selling him just as the coaching environment becomes more suitable would carry long-term risk.

The complication is Reece James. The captain’s fitness issues, including his current England hamstring concern, mean Chelsea still need genuine right-sided depth.

Palestra may be part of the future, but Gusto is already part of the present. Chelsea have too often needed emergency solutions in that area to treat proven depth lightly.

That is why Read Chelsea’s recent look at James awaiting hamstring scan results matters in this debate. Right-back remains one of the most talented areas of the squad, but also one of the least straightforward.

This is why Gusto’s France rhythm is more valuable than a simple tournament diary entry. Chelsea are watching a player operate inside an elite international camp, learn from a club legend and handle a public transfer valuation that can change his status overnight.

Alonso’s first decision does not have to be final, but it does have to be clear.

If Gusto stays, he needs a defined role early in pre-season and a pathway that makes sense alongside Palestra and James. If Chelsea sell, they need to do it at a number that justifies weakening one of the few squad areas where youth, speed and top-level experience already overlap.

Gusto’s Kante lesson, then, is not just a feel-good Chelsea connection. It is a reminder of the standard Alonso must demand before deciding whether £75million is a price to accept or a warning sign that Chelsea should hold firm.

“Even if Man United extend the contract, that might just be a protection so they can still sell him,” he added.

#2026. Man City's controversial orange kit sparks fan criticism as Premier League restriction emerges

By Naim Beneddra | 25 Nov 2026 17:29

Manchester City's decision to wear a striking orange fourth kit during Tuesday's Champions League draw with Napoli has drawn criticism from supporter groups, while Premier League regulations mean the controversial shirt cannot be worn in domestic league matches.

The Citizens abandoned their traditional sky-blue colours for the 2-2 encounter at the Etihad Stadium, unveiling a special-edition strip created by Puma and Italian fashion brand C.P. Company. The launch formed part of a commercial collaboration, with the collection scheduled to go on sale worldwide on December 1.

The decision proved divisive, particularly as Enzo Maresca's side extended their winless run to five matches. According to Brazilian outlet Trivela, two independent supporter organisations have questioned the timing of the initiative and its apparent prioritisation of commercial interests.

There is also a regulatory complication. City have already registered their three regular kits for the 2026-27 Premier League campaign, meaning their new orange design cannot be added to the selection of strips available for league fixtures.

Premier League clubs are permitted to use a maximum of three registered outfield kits during a season, normally comprising their home, away and third-choice designs.

City have already filled those places with their traditional sky-blue home strip, a black-and-yellow away kit and a predominantly white third shirt featuring blue detailing.

Their new orange shirt therefore falls outside the competition's permitted selection, regardless of whether the club wishes to use it again.

The restriction is not related to the unusual colour, the shirt's design or its association with C.P. Company. It follows from the fact that City have introduced an additional strip beyond the three registered for Premier League use.

UEFA's kit regulations operate differently, allowing clubs greater flexibility over the number of approved designs they can wear in European competition.

That explains why City were able to introduce the orange shirt against Napoli and could use it again during selected Champions League fixtures later this season.

The club confirmed the arrangement in an official statement following Tuesday's match, describing the outfit as a special-edition collaboration with Puma and C.P. Company.

Supporters will have an opportunity to purchase items from the collection at an exclusive pre-sale event at the City Store in Medlock Square on November 30, before the worldwide release the following day.

The FA Cup could also provide another opportunity to wear the shirt, although its use would require the appropriate approval from the Football Association.

Such exceptions are not unprecedented. Arsenal received permission to wear a special white kit in the FA Cup as part of their No More Red initiative, which raises awareness of knife crime and youth violence in London.

The Premier League restriction is only one aspect of the controversy surrounding City's latest release.

According to Trivela, the Citizens Trust, an independent supporter organisation established earlier this year, criticised the decision to introduce the orange shirt and expressed opposition to initiatives perceived as explicitly promoting third-party commercial interests.

Another independent organisation, the 1894 Group, reportedly raised concerns about the club's priorities, arguing that City should be concentrating on strengthening their relationship with supporters during a difficult period.

Those objections are particularly relevant because the new kit made its debut at the Etihad, where City would ordinarily wear their familiar sky-blue colours.

The orange design has no obvious connection to that traditional home identity. However, C.P. Company has a longstanding association with British football terrace fashion, and its partnership with City dates back to 2024.

The club therefore has a cultural and commercial rationale for the collaboration, even if some supporters remain unconvinced by its appearance on the pitch.

Tuesday's result did little to improve the mood. City were held to a 2-2 draw by Napoli despite a late equaliser from Rayan Cherki, extending a difficult run for Maresca and his players.

The manager is already facing questions about his team's performances ahead of Sunday's Premier League trip to Arsenal, making the timing of a high-profile commercial launch a particularly sensitive issue.

City have previously demonstrated that fourth kits can generate interest without provoking the same debate over their relationship with the club's identity.

Their 2024-25 special-edition shirt, developed with Noel Gallagher, celebrated the 30th anniversary of Oasis's debut album, Definitely Maybe.

The design drew on the Manchester band's history and Gallagher's longstanding support for City. It was worn during Champions League fixtures against Inter Milan and Paris Saint-Germain.

The latest collaboration follows a different approach, reflecting C.P. Company's influence on football fashion rather than a specific event from the club's own history.

There is no indication that City intend to withdraw the orange kit following the criticism. Their official announcement identifies it as an outfit for selected European fixtures, and the commercial launch remains scheduled for next week.

For supporters attending Premier League matches, however, there will be no repeat of Tuesday's unusual appearance. City's three registered domestic kits will remain the available options, leaving the orange shirt as a feature of their European campaign.

#2025. Middlesbrough dressing room insight reveals how players really feel about Southampton clash

Middlesbrough midfielder Myles Peart-Harris has said the Boro squad are "excited" for a "massive game" against Southampton on the South Coast on Friday night.

It's the first meeting between the two sides since the play-off semi-final last season, which Spygate ultimately overshadowed. Ahead of the first leg, Boro officials caught a Saints analyst illegally spying on their training session.

The games went ahead, and Southampton prevailed in extra time after neither side could be separated in 180 minutes. However, the EFL would then expel Tonda Eckert's side from the play-off final and reinstate Middlesbrough to take on Hull City at Wembley.

Since the Tigers beat Boro in May, the rivalry between both fanbases has been brewing, and was stoked by Eckert being handed just a fine and a suspended sentence by the FA for his role in the operation last month. A week ago, the German was given a new contract at St Mary's.

Fans of clubs can often play down the importance of games, or brand something as "their cup final, not ours," when discussing a matchup between two new rivals, but deep down, both Southampton and Middlesbrough fans want to get one over each other on Friday night.

Peart-Harris, who took his chance back in the starting eleven for the first time in two months on Tuesday night with a goal and an assist in the 2-1 win over Swansea City, watched on from home as Spygate played out back in May, given he wasn't at the club.

However, the 24-year-old knows how much the game means on Friday, and is looking forward to being a part of it.

"Everyone was watching it [the spygate developments]," he said, per the Northern Echo. "I remember watching the semi-final, and then I think maybe a week later you hear the news about what happened. So now, it's definitely something I’m excited to be a part of.

"Friday's a massive game. It’s a massive football game before anything else, but the rivalry that we now have makes it even better."

On how the rest of the team feels heading into the game, a lot of them will be looking to pick up a big win to almost right the wrongs of the last time they were down at St Mary's in the play-off semi-finals.

"The boys are looking forward to Friday. Hopefully we go down there, and we play our football, we stick to what we know and get a result," he continued.

"We're ready, and we’re excited. I think we're just more excited about the game of football that's going to actually happen, because we know they like to play football, we like to play football, so I think tactically it's going to be a great game for everyone to watch.

"Our aim will be to go down and just get a win."

As Peart-Harris said, first and foremost, though, Friday night will be a great chance for two of the best sides in the division to go head-to-head.

Kim Hellberg and Tonda Eckert are widely regarded as two of the brightest up-and-coming coaches in the game, and in the past year since they both took over at their respective clubs, the brand of football being played has been fascinating to watch.

This season, Boro have picked up 35 points, while Southampton have collected 32, albeit the four-point deduction handed to them in the wake of the Spygate charges by the EFL means that they sit on 28, seven points adrift of their visitors on Friday.

Who do you think comes out on top on Friday? Let us know in the comments.

The Teessiders have a tough task on their hands, with Eckert's side utterly dominant at home this season. The only side to have come away with any points from St Mary's through eight games were Bolton Wanderers, who needed to rely on a good defensive display to grind out a 0-0 draw.

It'll be intriguing to see if Boro set up any differently to help combat an unstoppable force on their home patch. Either way, these two teams, and both managers, are destined for the Premier League, and it'll be fun to see them lock horns for the first time this season.

Ultimately, this brooding rivalry is more to do with those in the stands, and perhaps the two coaches in the dugouts, too, even if both have since buried the hatchet after the incident back in May.

The Southampton players who were there to play out the semi-final in the play-offs last season likely weren't to know what Eckert and co were doing to help prepare them for games, and aren't really to blame. Taylor Harwood-Bellis, who was the pantomime villain, mocking Aidan Morris with a spy celebration after Shea Charles' winner, is no longer at the club.

They will be emotionally charged by the atmosphere inside St Mary's on Friday, though, which will have a little more of an edge to it. As mentioned earlier, both sets of fans may pretend not to care, but they do, deep down.

A red-hot atmosphere and two good footballing sides should create a wonderful cocktail of Championship football, and if both teams are up for it, it could go down as one of the games of the season already.

#2023. Senegal Knockout Tie Gives Chelsea A Double Xabi Alonso Audit

Chelsea’s World Cup watch has moved beyond simple minutes management.

Senegal’s Round-of-32 meeting with Belgium now gives Xabi Alonso a live read on two very different development tracks.

Nicolas Jackson’s attacking authority is the immediate question. Mamadou Sarr’s defensive readiness is the longer-term one.

Chelsea confirmed that Senegal kept their knockout hopes alive with a 5-0 win over Iraq, with Jackson featuring from the bench.

Senegal have since moved into the knockouts, where they face Belgium in Seattle on Wednesday 1 July.

Jackson and Sarr travel into that game with contrasting club questions attached. Both still carry clear Chelsea relevance.

Sarr has not been the loudest Chelsea story of the tournament.

That may actually make this test more useful.

The 20-year-old is still moving through the awkward stage between prospect, squad option and saleable BlueCo asset.

Knockout football against Belgium can sharpen that picture quickly.

The timing is awkward in the best possible way for Chelsea. Alonso steps into a squad with expensive centre-backs, academy-trained options and a board still comfortable using the market aggressively.

A disciplined Sarr performance would not end that search. It would give the recruitment team a stronger internal benchmark before committing another major fee.

Chelsea need defenders who can hold a high line, cover wide channels and still pass through pressure. Belgium should ask those questions more directly than most group-stage opponents.

The context matters. Chelsea’s recall note recorded that Sarr returned from Strasbourg after 18 appearances across Ligue 1 and the Conference League.

The club also noted that he had already earned his first Senegal cap.

That is a different profile from an academy player being carried by reputation alone.

There is a statistical base behind the interest too. When Chelsea completed the deal, beIN SPORTS noted that Sarr made 27 Ligue 1 appearances in his first Strasbourg season.

He helped them keep 10 clean sheets. He also produced a team-high 20 blocks and made 25 interceptions.

Those numbers explain why this Belgium game is not just a sentimental World Cup subplot.

Alonso’s Chelsea defence is already crowded with Levi Colwill, Wesley Fofana, Trevoh Chalobah and external targets still orbiting the plan.

ReadChelsea has already looked at why Colwill’s recovery story gives Alonso a defensive test. Sarr’s value is different.

He is still being evaluated as a physical, long-range defensive project.

Jackson’s role changes the editorial weight of the tie.

Chelsea’s striker picture has been messy for long enough. Any high-pressure international performance now carries wider meaning.

ReadChelsea has already framed the forward’s tournament as a live Senegal striker audit for Chelsea.

Belgium should stress both Chelsea questions at once.

Jackson has to offer penalty-box threat and transition security. Sarr, if trusted, has to defend space against a technically cleaner opponent.

He also has to show whether his recovery pace and frame translate under tournament pressure.

Sarr’s own mentality profile is worth noting. In a Chelsea World Cup Q&A, he named Lionel Messi, Sadio Mane and Paul Pogba as his tournament heroes.

His answer on Pogba focused on leadership and group mentality.

That lands neatly with the exact question Chelsea now need answered. Is there enough presence to go with the physical tools?

This is why Belgium feels like a pathway check rather than a routine World Cup watch item.

Chelsea do not need Sarr to solve the defence in July. They need clearer evidence on whether he belongs in Alonso’s first pre-season group.

He could need another loan. He could also become part of the club’s centre-back market arithmetic.

For Jackson, the question is more immediate. For Sarr, it is more structural.

Together, Senegal’s knockout tie gives Chelsea a rare clean look at two players on opposite ends of the same squad-building argument.

Who can help now, and who is worth protecting for later?

#2022. Mauricio Pochettino proud of Man Utd response after chaotic Sporting draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Mauricio Pochettino praised Manchester United’s character and spirit after his side earned a 2-2 draw against Sporting Lisbon in the Champions League on Wednesday night.

Manchester United travelled to Portugal looking to respond after their disappointing 4-0 defeat to Liverpool in Pochettino’s first game in charge. They showed a much-improved performance away from home, although a number of costly moments prevented the new head coach from celebrating his first victory.

Bruno Fernandes scored twice on his return to the stadium, putting United ahead after Luis Suarez had given Sporting the lead. The home striker also scored twice, including a penalty, to ensure the points were shared.

Senne Lammens had earlier saved a Suarez spot-kick, but the United goalkeeper was involved in another penalty incident that allowed the Sporting forward to find the net. United also had two goals ruled out, while Youri Tielemans was sent off late in the contest.

Despite the frustrating outcome, Pochettino was pleased with what he saw from his players.

The Argentine felt United controlled large periods of the first half but were disappointed to go into the break behind.

“The first half I think we controlled the game, I think we dominated. We were disappointed to finish the first half 1-0 down,” Pochettino said after the game.

“The second half I think we started the way we want to play. That is the style, the character. That is the way we want to establish and feeling this way we can perform.”

The United boss also made it clear that he was proud of the players despite the decisions and incidents that went against them.

“I’m so proud of my players. I think it was a very tough game. I liked the team during 90 minutes, in the way that we showed personality and character.”

Pochettino felt his side had been punished repeatedly in important moments.

“In the second half, we recovered a lot of balls in the opposite half. We were aggressive and I think that is the way we want to play,” he added.

After the heavy defeat at Anfield, United showed a different level of fight in Lisbon. The draw will still feel like a missed opportunity for Manchester United and Pochettino. However, the Argentine manager believes his players are beginning to embrace the style of play he wants.

#2021. Michael Olise Gives Chelsea A Hard Xabi Alonso Wide-Player Benchmark

Michael Olise has moved from former Chelsea academy footnote to genuine Ballon d’Or conversation piece.

That should land at Cobham as more than a wistful what-if.

Bavarian Football Works relayed comments from former Chelsea and France winger Florent Malouda, who described the Bayern Munich forward as a live Ballon d’Or contender if France turn their World Cup run into silverware.

The point is not simply that Chelsea once had Olise in the building. It is that his rise provides a hard benchmark for the type of final-third specialist Xabi Alonso must either develop or buy.

Chelsea have spent heavily on attacking upside. Olise’s rise is a reminder that the real separator is not potential, but repeatable elite production.

Bayern’s own season review underlines why the conversation has accelerated.

The German champions confirmed Olise finished 2025/26 with 25 goals and 28 assists from 57 competitive appearances. He was also named Bayern’s Player of the Season for a second straight year, finishing ahead of Harry Kane and Luis Diaz in the fan vote.

That is elite production from a wide creator who does not play like a chalk-on-boots winger.

Olise can receive wide, drift inside, manipulate the half-space and still deliver the last pass at match-winning speed. Chelsea have bought attackers with pieces of that profile, but few have joined the dots with that level of consistency.

There is an obvious recruitment lesson here.

The market will now price that blend of creativity, ball security and goal threat close to superstar territory. If Chelsea want an Alonso attack that controls games rather than simply runs through them, the next winger decision cannot be judged on pace or upside alone.

It has to be judged on game control, final-third calm and repeat output.

The World Cup has sharpened the comparison.

Chelsea’s official France preview noted that Malo Gusto was in the same squad as N’Golo Kante and former Blues academy player Olise. It was a neat reminder of how many Chelsea strands sit inside Didier Deschamps’ group.

Bundesliga’s tournament analysis was even more revealing. It noted that Olise had reached 10 France goal involvements in 18 appearances and was named Player of the Match on his World Cup debut against Senegal.

That same analysis highlighted the tactical adjustment that moved Olise closer to Kylian Mbappe after half-time. His passing range then became the lever that changed the game.

That matters for Chelsea because Alonso’s best Bayer Leverkusen sides were built around players who could receive between lines without killing the tempo. Cole Palmer already gives Chelsea one version of that.

The wider question is whether the squad has another attacker who can bend matches from the opposite side without turning every possession into a one-v-one.

Olise’s Chelsea connection can easily become sentimental. The sharper editorial point is structural.

This is the calibre of player who once passed through the academy pathway and later became a global-level attacker elsewhere. Chelsea cannot change that history, but they can use it to stress-test the next wave of decisions.

That matters for Tyrique George, Kendry Paez, Estevao and any new wide signing.

ReadChelsea has already looked at how Tyrique George’s likely Everton return gives Alonso a fresh winger audit. Olise’s current standing raises the standard of that discussion.

He is no longer just a former academy name or a missed Premier League target. He is evidence of what happens when technical security, role clarity and patience meet elite coaching.

For Alonso, that is the real value of the Olise noise.

Chelsea do not need to turn every academy exit into regret. They do need to recognise the standard now being set by one of Cobham’s former pupils.

The next wide forward who gets serious minutes at Stamford Bridge has to be assessed against that ceiling, not against the safer language of potential.

#2020. Nottingham Forest vs. Chelsea: Oliver Glasner claims his team, Blues share similar issues ahead of City Ground showdown

By Darren Plant | 25 Nov 2026 17:37

Oliver Glasner has outlined how Nottingham Forest must improve during Friday's Premier League fixture against Chelsea.

However, there is an argument that Xabi Alonso's Blues are suffering from the same issues as their East Midlands hosts.

Forest head into their upcoming game sitting in 17th position in the Premier League table, seven points adrift of their eighth-placed opponents.

While Forest's underlying statistics in the final third have been encouraging, individual mistakes have sometimes cost them.

Speaking at his pre-match press conference, Glasner acknowledged that Forest's players must perform better in many areas of their game.

The Austrian told reporters: "We’ve been talking a lot about running here. You can see we’ve climbed the bottom of the table. We are starting to be in mid-table with all these stats. I think we can see this progression.

"The physical – especially the running stats – are getting more and more competitive. You don’t need to be a heavyweight boxer in our squad. But you need to anticipate. You need to be quick in legs and in mind."

When addressing other issues, he added: "We all thought and wished we would have more points. Performances in many games were better than results. We wanted and expected more from us to be honest.

"We have to accept the reality. I’m no magician. I have no magic dust I can spread. We need to improve our individual mistakes.

"We need to improve our efficiency in attack. Still no goals from set pieces. I think I always talked about stability and consistency. This is what we still have to find and do better.

"To really perform consistently on a high level, we get enough points and we get enough wins. Then you climb the table and this is what we’re looking for."

After the 4-1 defeat at home to Leeds United, Alonso gave a dismissive answer when a reporter claimed that Chelsea were unable to cope physically against the majority of Premier League teams.

However, it has been clear to supporters that Chelsea possess a team that struggle on that front, with decision-making and in front of goal.

Add in the frequent individual mistakes and you get a similar team to Forest, albeit one that is less physically-imposing.

There is an argument that Chelsea are the perfect opponents for Forest to return to form.

#2019. Jeff Stelling made QPR prediction in August that he might've misjudged

Jeff Stelling had backed QPR for relegation from the Championship, as they now chase promotion to the Premier League.

It has been a positive campaign so far for the R’s, who are in the play-off places, and they have played some good football under Julien Stephan.

This has come on the back of an inconsistent first season under the Frenchman, which ultimately saw QPR finish 15th.

Therefore, it was hard to predict exactly how QPR would perform this season. Whilst there had been some encouraging signs under Stephan, the Londoners lost eight of their last 13 games in the previous season, which included four straight defeats at the end.

So, there was frustration among the supporters that they couldn’t build on a decent start, and many wondered whether they would take that negative momentum into the 2026/27 season.

And, it clearly influenced Stelling, with the TalkSPORT presenter and former Soccer Saturday host predicting relegation for QPR back in August.

It should be said that Stelling made it clear he had no problem with the R’s, and he even admitted he would ‘offend’ their fans, and that he ‘hopes’ he would be wrong.

Pleasingly for QPR, it looks like he will be, as Stephan’s side are 16 points clear of the bottom three, and they’re just seven points behind leaders Middlesbrough, so they are certainly focusing on the other end of the table right now.

We all make calls that are way off, and that looks to be the case with Stelling and QPR here.

A look at the amount of pubs within a two-mile radius of every Championship ground this season.

The Championship is notoriously difficult, and that unpredictability is why it’s such an enthralling league, even if it was a bold shout to say the R’s could be relegated.

Nevertheless, they are having a good season so far, and most fans would’ve snapped your hand off to be in the play-offs right now, particularly with the way the team are playing.

That’s probably the most encouraging aspect of everything for the R’s, as it doesn’t feel like they’re in a false position, and, if anything, they maybe deserve to be higher in the table.

All credit for that goes to Stephan, as he has built a stylish, attacking side that has proven they can compete with the best teams in this league.

So, the focus for QPR will be to make Stelling’s prediction look even worse come May, when they will hope to be back in the Premier League.

That may seem like a long shot when you consider the resources of the clubs they are competing against, but, as outlined above, they are a very good Championship team right now.

Therefore, January will be crucial, and the R’s may need reinforcements if they are to make this season a truly memorable one.

#2018. Pochettino fumes at ‘impossible’ decisions after Man Utd draw with Sporting

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Mauricio Pochettino was left frustrated by several refereeing decisions during Manchester United’s 2-2 draw with Sporting Lisbon, insisting his side were punished in key moments of the game.

Manchester United had to settle for a point in Portugal after a chaotic evening away from home. Bruno Fernandes scored twice, but Luis Suarez also found the net on two occasions to deny Pochettino his first win as United head coach.

Referee Szymon Marciniak came under scrutiny for a series of controversial decisions. He ruled out an Amad Diallo goal after penalising Youri Tielemans for a foul in the build-up, blowing his whistle before the ball crossed the line. That meant VAR could not intervene on the goal and it was not given.

The Polish official also awarded Sporting a first-half penalty and disallowed what would have been a late United winner. He blew whistle on Patrick Dorgu for pushing goalkeeper Kaique Pereira, disallowing the goal.

Pochettino did not hide his frustration when asked about the decisions after the match.

“I think your first question is difficult to answer because I think everyone knows all that was, it was impossible,” he said after the game.

“You know, it’s difficult to understand why that happened on the field. It’s difficult to find a game where you get punished [so much] in one game. We were punished a lot in key actions.”

United also conceded an 80th-minute equaliser after Senne Lammens failed to hold a cross, allowing Suarez to score from close range.

However, Pochettino was keen to defend his goalkeeper rather than blame him for the mistake.

“I think, please, it’s to blame us,” the United boss said.

He acknowledged that his side needed to manage such situations better.

“But of course, we need to accept, we accept the challenge and we are here to try to improve, you know, the small details that for sure we will improve.”

Pochettino’s frustration was understandable after two similar incidents of bad refereeing in his first two games at United. Leny Yoro’s red card against Liverpool was later overturned on appeal, while a series of errors against Sporting cost him yet another victory. But despite the errors, Pochettino also recognises that his players need to improve their decision-making during games.

#2017. Chelsea Could Claim Share of £164m Compensation

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Chelsea fans have followed Manchester City’s financial rules case for years. Everyone at Stamford Bridge knows a guilty verdict could cost City serious money, and much of it could end up with rivals lower down the table.

Reports now suggest an independent panel has reached a decision. If that holds up, the scale of any Chelsea Manchester City compensation payout could run into tens of millions of pounds for the Blues alone.

The Premier League charged Manchester City with 115 alleged breaches of financial regulations in February 2023. That followed a four-year investigation into the club’s accounts. The case has hung over the competition ever since. Every top-flight club knows the outcome could change how recent Premier League seasons are remembered.

This matters directly to Chelsea. If City are found to have broken spending and disclosure rules, clubs who finished below them could argue they lost out on prize money and European qualification. Chelsea have competed directly with City for top four places and cup competitions throughout that period.

According to The Athletic, Manchester City were found guilty on all but one of the 115 charges by the independent panel. The Premier League has not confirmed the verdict or issued a statement.

Manchester City have not accepted any suggestion of guilt. The club released a statement addressing the ongoing process directly.

“The Premier League process remains ongoing, with significant elements to be completed, and subject to strict confidentiality. As such, Manchester City FC’s position remains consistent with the Club’s statement of February 2023. ‘The Club has diligently respected due process for eight years on the basis that the Premier League Board and Executive would behave as an independent, impartial and fair-minded regulator, free from partisan influence.'”

That statement makes clear the club does not see the case as settled. Until the Premier League confirms a result, the reported verdict will linger on for official sentencing.

Data specialists at OLBG have estimated the potential Chelsea Manchester City compensation windfall if City are found guilty. They believe City could owe domestic rivals well over £500m in lost Premier League prize money if the charges are proven.

Within that total, London clubs are said to be due around £164m between them. Chelsea, Arsenal and Tottenham Hotspur are among the clubs who could be entitled to a share. Other Premier League sides could benefit too, depending on where they finished compared with City in the seasons in question.

Prize money in the Premier League is tied closely to league position. If City gained points unfairly, that would have pushed a rival down the table and cost them money too. That is the basis for the OLBG figures, and it explains why the sums involved are so large.

For Chelsea, even a share of that £164m pot would be a big boost. It could help fund new signings, or simply ease the pressure around the kind of transfer valuations at Stamford Bridge the club faces every window.

City chairman Khaldoon Al Mubarak has already made clear the case is far from finished. He described the process as having “a long way to run.” That points to appeals and further legal steps, whatever the panel has decided.

Chelsea, for their part, carry on with life under Xabi Alonso regardless of how the case unfolds. Attention stays on squad rotation and Xabi Alonso’s squad management strategy, not on any potential windfall. The financial case will not change anything on the pitch in the short term.

The Premier League has stayed silent on the reported verdict, and confidentiality remains strict around the process, according to reports from Football.London. Chelsea fans should expect a long wait before any compensation, if it comes at all, reaches the club’s accounts.

#2015. Bruno Fernandes tells players what to do next after Sporting draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bruno Fernandes has urged Manchester United to stick together and fight for their season after they were held to a 2-2 draw by Sporting Lisbon in the Champions League.

The United captain scored twice on his return to the stadium, putting Manchester United ahead after Luis Suarez had given Sporting the lead. The home striker also scored twice, including a penalty, to ensure the points were shared.

Senne Lammens had earlier saved a Suarez spot-kick, but the United goalkeeper was involved in another penalty incident that allowed the Sporting forward to find the net. United also had two goals ruled out after some controversial decisions, while Youri Tielemans was sent off late in the contest.

It was another frustrating result for United, who are still searching for their first win under Mauricio Pochettino. Fernandes believes there were positives in the performance, but insists the team must now focus on getting results.

Speaking to TNT Sports after the match, Fernandes revealed that Pochettino wanted his players to show more aggression after the break.

“[At half-time the manager] wanted more aggression. more bodies. The first goal comes from that.”

The Portuguese midfielder felt United had controlled much of the first half despite conceding. He admitted they needed more presence in the final third.

“Obviously, I think we started the game well. We gave a penalty away where Senne [Lammens] saved it. We gave one or two counters, but we were very good in recovery positions.

“They got the goal, but we were still in control of the game. We didn’t have much people in the last third. Second half we got the two goals. That was the moment we should push a bit more.”

United’s inability to hold on to their lead was another setback, but Fernandes refused to single out individuals for the mistakes.

“Mistakes are part of football and from that you get goals. Everyone has to take the blame for everything we do good and we do bad as well. We need to be together in the good and in the bad moments.”

“We need points to get through. and we need to focus on the next one. Now the moment is to get back to the Premier League. We need to stick together and fight for our lives.”

#2014. Jamie Gittens suffers Chelsea injury blow with England U21s

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Gittens has withdrawn from the England Under-21 squad. He suffered an ankle injury during Tuesday’s qualifier against Kazakhstan at Molineux. The Chelsea winger left the ground on crutches. He has returned to Stamford Bridge, where the Jamie Gittens injury will now be assessed by the club’s medical staff.

Gittens picked up the knock after a challenge during the second half of England’s win over Kazakhstan. He was withdrawn as a precaution. He did not take any further part in the match. England have been careful not to overstate the issue and no further reports have come out further.

The 21-year-old struggled to put weight on the affected ankle and was seen limping around Molineux after full time. That prompted an immediate release back to his club, rather than any attempt to assess him further in camp.

England Under-21 boss Lee Carsley addressed the injury directly after the game. He confirmed the nature of the knock and the decision to send Gittens home.

“Jamie got a kick on his ankle,” Carsley said after the match. “We never take any risks. With the age of these players, we don’t ever keep them in camp to see how they are. He will go back.”

England’s statement on the squad changes, detailed in a report from Football.London, confirmed the wider fallout from the fixture.

“Mukasa’s call-up follows the withdrawal of Chelsea’s Jamie Gittens and Norwich City pair Kellen Fisher and Ben Chrisene through injury,” the statement read. West Ham midfielder Divine Mukasa has been called up as cover for all three.

Gittens has been an unused substitute in every Premier League fixture Chelsea have played this season, with his only two appearances under Xabi Alonso coming in the Carabao Cup. Those minutes have offered him little platform after his big summer move.

Alonso’s squad selection has been a talking point since he took charge. Our earlier look at Xabi Alonso’s squad choices at Stamford Bridge covered how competitive things have become. An ankle injury will not change his pecking order in the short term. It does, though, remove any chance of him pushing his case this week.

Chelsea’s break has brought other headlines too. Cole Palmer also withdrew, for entirely different reasons, after his recall by senior boss Thomas Tuchel to the full England squad. Chelsea will be watching both returns closely before domestic football resumes.

Chelsea have not yet issued a formal update on the severity of the injury. Any recovery timeline will depend on an assessment by the club’s medical team once Gittens is back at Cobham. Nothing beyond the crutches seen at Molineux has been confirmed.

Given his limited game time this season, the immediate concern is less about missed fixtures than a setback to his integration under Alonso. Fans can follow how the squad shapes up before the next fixtures through our recent line-up updates. Further news on Gittens is expected once Chelsea’s medical staff finish their evaluation.

#2013. Alanyaspor vs Konyaspor - prediction, team news, lineups

By Joshua Cole | 25 Nov 2026 18:32

Alanyaspor will be looking to halt their alarming slide down the Turkish Super Lig standings when they welcome Konyaspor to GAIN Park on Friday evening.

The hosts sit 14th in the table with 12 points from 12 matches, while the visitors occupy 15th place with eight points and will be hoping to close the gap after suffering three consecutive league defeats.

Alanyaspor have endured a difficult period in recent weeks, losing five consecutive league matches and leaving themselves increasingly vulnerable to being dragged into the relegation battle.

The Thunders began that sequence with a 3-0 defeat away to Fenerbahce before suffering a 2-1 home loss against Kocaelispor six days later, and their struggles continued when they travelled to face Genclerbirligi, suffering a humiliating 5-0 defeat, which was followed by a 2-0 home loss to Trabzonspor.

Those results led to Joao Pereira leaving by mutual agreement, with Ugur Ucar subsequently appointed, but the new manager was unable to bring about an immediate improvement as Alanyaspor were beaten 2-1 at Kasimpasa in his first game in charge.

They have now lost five Super Lig matches in a row and are without a win in their last six, drawing one, leaving Ucar with plenty to address as his side attempt to rediscover the form that helped them collect points earlier in the campaign.

Their recent performances have also been particularly disappointing defensively, having conceded 12 goals across their last five league matches, making them the worst-performing side in the division over that period.

The 4-1 victory over 12 Bingolspor in the Turkish Cup is the only positive result the Thunders have recorded during that spell, and just their second home win across all competitions, having won only their opening home league game, drawn two and lost two, leaving them without a league victory on home soil in their last four attempts.

However, Alanyaspor have enjoyed the better of recent meetings between the sides, remaining unbeaten in their last six competitive encounters with Konyaspor while winning each of the last five.

Konyaspor, meanwhile, have also struggled to maintain consistency this season and are four points behind the hosts, having collected just two wins, two draws and eight defeats from their opening 12 league matches.

Ilhan Palut's side have also lost their last three league games, suffering a heavy 4-0 home defeat against Galatasaray before losing 1-0 away to Samsunspor and then 4-2 against Besiktas in their latest outing.

Aside from their poor record in this fixture, the Anatolian Eagles have another concern heading into Friday's encounter, having collected just one point from five away matches this season.

In fact, Konyaspor have failed to score in their last four away matches, losing three of those games by a 1-0 scoreline, with a goalless draw against Kasimpasa coming between those defeats.

The Anatolian side have also failed to win on their last three visits to Alanyaspor, losing the previous two by 2-1 scorelines after drawing 2-2 on their earlier trip.

Alanyaspor will be without Fidan Aliti, who is suspended for Friday's encounter, leaving Ucar with a defensive selection concern ahead of the match.

Fatih Aksoy and Paolo Fernandes are also carrying fitness concerns, with both players reported as injury doubts, although their availability could be assessed closer to kick-off.

Ruan and Hwang Ui-jo are expected to feature in attack, while Ianis Hagi and Gaïus Makouta could provide creativity from midfield as Alanyaspor look to improve their scoring record.

Konyaspor will be without Adil Demirbag, who is recovering from an injury and is expected to remain sidelined, while Chidozie Awaziem is also a doubt.

Palut could therefore retain much of the side that started against Besiktas, with Enis Destan and Jackson Muleka expected to provide the attacking threat, while Deniz Turuc and Jean-Luc Dompe could play important roles in supporting the forwards.

Victor; Hadergjonaj, Lima, Ozyurt, Ozdemir; Makouta, Maestro; Kaya, Hagi, Ruan; Hwang Ui-jo

Gungordu; Oguz, Yazgili, Bosluk, Masuaku; Turuc, Toth, Ibrahimoglu, Dompe; Destan, Muleka

Alanyaspor are struggling to produce consistent performances at both ends of the pitch, but their home advantage could prove important against a Konyaspor side that has also experienced a difficult run of results.

However, the home side’s defensive problems should give the away side’s opportunities to find the net, and with both teams desperate to avoid another defeat, we expect a closely contested encounter to finish level.

#2012. Asmir Begovic reveals two things Stoke City must do to secure promotion

Stoke City have continued their incredible run of form under Mark Robins with a 2-1 home victory over Millwall on Wednesday night to send them joint top of the Championship alongside Middlesbrough.

Everything is rosy right now in the Potteries for Stoke, who can’t stop winning at the moment, with their most recent victory over Millwall extending their winning streak to five games on the spin.

The last time the Potters were on such a run was back in 2008 with Tony Pulis at the helm; they would go on to win promotion to the Premier League that year, as now with Robins in the dugout, Stoke supporters will be hoping history can repeat itself.

However, there is still a long way to go, and the club cannot afford to rest on their laurels or become complacent anytime soon, as one former fan favourite has revealed the two things they must do to secure a Premier League return this season.

Football League World takes a closer look at the best value-for-money signings in the Championship this season

Although Stoke sit in the automatic promotion places in the Championship right now, a mere ten points separates them from 12th place; as for how good things have been, it only takes a poor couple of results to go back to reality.

Former goalkeeper and fan favourite Asmir Begovic, speaking to The Sentinel, has now revealed the two things the Potters must do this year to fire their way back to the top flight.

Begovic said: "It's not easy to get out of the Championship, but it's good to see them having a positive start. Listen, there's still a long way to go. Now we're getting into the meat and bones of the season going into the Christmas period, and it's really important to try to continue being consistent.

"They've obviously made some really good signings as well, Ethan Galbraith has been absolutely outstanding, a great signing. So for some of the things that have gone wrong, you've got to give the board and Jon Walters credit for strengthening the squad.

"Now it's about keeping that consistency, maybe adding the latter piece or two in January given their resources and positioning. The fact that the play-offs have also extended has given a lot of teams a lot more hope as well."

Do you agree with Begovic's take on the matter? What do you think Stoke must do to secure promotion? Share your thoughts below!

Begovic, 39, has had an exceptional career in professional football, beginning his career with Portsmouth before spells with Macclesfield, Bournemouth, Yeovil Town, Stoke, Chelsea, Qarabağ, AC Milan, Everton, Queen’s Park Rangers, and Leicester City.

It would be with the Potters where he would play the most games in his career, making 173 appearances across all competitions in North Staffordshire, having joined the club from Portsmouth in January 2010 for around £3.25 million, and eventually being sold to Chelsea for around £8 million.

Speaking to The Sentinel, the Bosnian has been full of praise for current Stoke number one and Sweden international Viktor Johansson, saying: "I think he certainly does have Premier League potential. I think you can see now he's being very selective with his next move. I think he wants to continue to play.

"I'm sure he would have had opportunities to go in as a number two at times, but I think the fact that he wants to play and be number one for club and country shows a lot about his character and his mentality. I think he definitely has the capabilities and talent to play in the Premier League."

Johansson penned a fresh four-year deal at the bet365 Stadium over the summer, having initially joined the club from Rotherham United in the summer of 2024 for just over £1 million, and has been exceptional ever since.

Certainly, Johansson will be hoping to play Premier League football one day, and at this rate, there is no reason it can’t be with Stoke.

Robins has been in similar positions before, but to date, has not quite gotten over the line, at least not in the Championship, falling short with Coventry City and suffering a major dip in form last year with Stoke.

The 56-year-old will be well aware of the position he is in, what he has to do, and how to do it, but should still listen to the advice the former Bosnian international has laid out before him.

The January window presents Stoke with an opportunity to add one or two fresh bodies to the mix, and if they are to get over the line, those additions could be pivotal in their year, as transfer plans must be in place ahead of the coming months, whilst remaining consistent has to be the priority for now.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

#2011. Ashley Young tears into referee after Manchester United denied dramatic late winner

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United had to settle for a point in Portugal after a chaotic evening away from home.

Bruno Fernandes scored twice, but Luis Suarez also found the net on two occasions to deny Pochettino his first win as United head coach.

Referee Szymon Marciniak came under scrutiny for a series of controversial decisions. He ruled out an Amad Diallo goal after penalising Youri Tielemans for a foul in the build-up, blowing his whistle before the ball crossed the line. That meant VAR could not intervene on the goal and it was not given.

The Polish official also awarded Sporting a first-half penalty and disallowed what would have been a late United winner. He blew whistle on Patrick Dorgu for pushing goalkeeper Kaique Pereira, disallowing the goal.

The decisions left plenty of frustration among Manchester United supporters, but former footballer Ashley Young called the officiating disgraceful.

Young questioned several decisions made by Marciniak, particularly the disallowed Amad goal and the late incident involving Dorgu.

“The referee’s performance was just simply wow. The decisions that he gave, including the one for what should have been Amad’s equaliser, with Tielemans, were unbelievable,” Ashley Young said while talking to BBC.

“Tielemans getting booked, which should never have been a booking, and then eventually getting sent off later. It should have been an equalising goal.

“Then the third goal as well, which should have stood, because he’s saying there’s a foul, whether he’s saying Lisandro Martinez or Patrick Dorgu fouled the keeper. The keeper comes out and flaps at it before he’s even touched.

“How that has gone to VAR is beyond me. His decision-making today is nothing more than a disgrace.”

The disallowed goals proved particularly costly, with Pochettino’s side missing the chance to take all three points despite Fernandes’ brace.

Mauricio Pochettino is still without a win after replacing Michael Carrick. Manchester United faces Brentford next with a hope of a first win for Pochettino.

#2010. Manu Koné: Roma slap £86m price tag on Chelsea target

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Roma have put an £86m (€100m) price tag on Manu Koné, according to Gazzetta dello Sport, reviving the Manu Koné Chelsea transfer link that first emerged over the summer. Roma want any interested club, including Chelsea, to know the price before serious talks can even begin.

Koné remains under contract at the Stadio Olimpico, and he has become a key part of Roma’s midfield since his move from Borussia Mönchengladbach. The Gazzetta report reads like a warning to potential buyers rather than proof of any talks between the two clubs.

Roma sporting director Tony D’Amico has confirmed Chelsea made contact late in the summer window, though he was quick to draw a clear line under how far things went.

“There was an inquiry and some preliminary contact with Chelsea on the final day of the transfer window. No official offers arrived that would allow us to approach the player. Something was imagined that simply didn’t happen. There was no official offer; the final day of the transfer window got everyone a little worked up.”

D’Amico’s comments confirm Chelsea looked at signing Koné before the deadline, but they stop well short of showing an official bid was ever made. He also explained why Roma had no wish to sell a key player so close to the window closing.

“Before the match against Fiorentina (on 24 August), I said it would be difficult for us to deprive ourselves of important players so near to the end of the transfer session, and so it proved. Kone is an important player, we’re happy he stayed with us, and we’re confident he’ll give his contribution during the season.”

Chelsea’s midfield has changed shape under Xabi Alonso since the summer, with Morgan Rogers arriving to add depth and competition for places. That signing came during a window that also saw exits acceleration under Xabi Alonso, as the head coach reshaped his squad around his own style.

Koné’s ball winning and his range of progressive passing would suit Alonso well, should Chelsea ever revisit their interest. Supporters have already seen how Alonso rotates his midfield for the biggest games, including the recent derby line-up selections against Brentford.

For now, Rogers and Chelsea’s existing central options give Alonso enough cover without needing an immediate signing. Roma’s reluctance to sell also means any deal would need patience rather than a quick resolution.

Nothing in D’Amico’s comments suggests Roma are open to a sale in the near term, and Koné has given no sign that he wants to leave. The £86m valuation, reported by Football London, stands as Roma’s marker rather than an asking price involved with Chelsea.

Will Chelsea return with a more formal approach in a future window? D’Amico’s comments give no hint either way. The Manu Koné Chelsea transfer link is unlikely to fade given Chelsea’s history of scouting France’s leading midfielders, but any fresh move will have to wait for Roma’s stance to soften.

#2009. Former winger wants selfish Bruno Fernandes to be sold and identifies replacement

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Despite a slow start to last season, Bruno Fernandes went on to finish the last season with 21 Premier League assists, breaking the competition’s single-season record.

This season has been a different situation. He isn’t at his usual best, contract talks have reported stalled and he publicy questioned Carrick’s tactics.

Bruno Fernandes has come under criticism for his performances and Ashley Young joins in as the latest to call for Bruno’s sale in January.

Manchester United have been told to cash in on Bruno Fernandes in January rather than offer the midfielder another contract. Meanwhile, Young identifies Morgan Gibbs-White as an upgrade over the Portuguese star.

The criticism of Fernandes has been particularly strong, with questions raised over his influence in central midfield.

“If Manchester United can cash in on him in January, I’d do that. I wouldn’t be looking to extend his contract at all. Keep him there, he’d be unhappy,” Ashley Young said while talking to BBC.

Young also questioned Fernandes’ attitude towards individual recognition, suggesting that winning personal awards appears to be an important part to his United career.

“He seems to me as a player that personal accolades are more important than the team. And that’s what he’s about. He’ll be looking at that, yeah, I want player of the year. I won player of the year. It was, it was me. You know, it weren’t my other teammates.”

The concerns were not limited to Fernandes’ attitude. His defensive positioning and the spaces he leaves behind when playing in central midfield were also highlighted.

The criticism comes from a view that opponents have regularly found opportunities to play through United’s midfield, particularly when Fernandes steps forward.

“I was at Everton teams, playing against Manchester United, and if he’s playing in central midfield, the tactic was, play through the midfield when you see them step out because there’s so much space in there. And it’s been the theme. It’s been the theme for years that Manchester United, if they play him in central midfield, there’s spaces all behind him.”

Ashley Young’s suggested alternative is Nottingham Forest’s Morgan Gibbs-White, who is believed to offer a different balance to the United midfield.

“I would go and get Morgan Gibbs-White if you can. And I’d go and do it in January. For me, I think Morgan Gibbs-White would give more to that team than what Bruno Fernandes is now.”

It would be a major decision for United if they let him go. There is no doubt on his quality as he has scored 113 goals in 341 appearances since arriving from Sporting in 2020.

However, his future has become a talking point after United reportedly saw two contract offers rejected. Fernandes is understood to want a four-year agreement, while the club remain cautious about committing to such a deal with the midfielder already 32.

With Mauricio Pochettino in the dugout now and reportedly involved in his contract negotiations, the upcoming months can be very different for Manchester United and Bruno Fernandes.

#2008. Joao Pedro Injury: Chelsea Forward Misses Brentford Defeat

Graeme Hanna is the site lead of Read Rangers and also writes about West Ham for Read West Ham. A Rangers season-ticket holder and freelance writer, he has previously written for Vital Rangers, Rangers Review, Football Ground Guide and Give Me Sport, and has a long association with the Follow Follow fanzine.

Joao Pedro missed Chelsea’s 3-0 Premier League defeat to Brentford with a knee problem, and the injury has since ruled him out of Brazil’s squad for the friendlies against Australia and India.

He was absent from Xabi Alonso’s confirmed lineups against Brentford, with Chelsea turning to other forward options instead.

Brazil’s coaching staff confirmed the withdrawal, pointing to the same knee issue that kept him out of the derby. Chelsea have not set a firm return date for Joao Pedro, leaving supporters to wait for further updates during the international break.

The Joao Pedro injury situation took a new turn when the Standard reported that he is seeing a specialist during the international break to speed up his recovery.

According to the Standard, the forward is using this pause in the calendar to get ahead of the problem rather than wait for Chelsea to resume domestic action.

Chelsea have not issued their own statement on the specialist visit, and the club’s official line on Joao Pedro’s fitness remains limited to his withdrawal from matchday duty.

Full detail on the reported treatment can be found in football.london’s reported injury update, which first carried the specialist claim alongside Xabi Alonso’s comments on the striker’s season.

Until Chelsea confirm otherwise, the Standard’s report stands as the clearest picture of how the club are handling the knee concern.

It suggests a measured approach, with recovery prioritised over rushing Joao Pedro back into contention for the next fixture.

Before the injury forced him out, Xabi Alonso had made his admiration for Joao Pedro clear. The Chelsea manager ranked the forward among the Premier League’s elite strikers, alongside Manchester City’s Erling Haaland.

“If you count how many top strikers we have in the Premier League, I’m sure that João is there, Haaland for sure is there.

“So I would rate him that way. He has a very strong goal this year: to have a great season, to score many goals, and to be a nominee for not just this month but more months. I am not surprised, but really happy with the kind of player that we have here with João.”

With Joao Pedro sidelined, Alonso must lean again on the depth built into his squad.

His approach to rotation, part of the squad management this season, faces another test while his in-form forward is unavailable.

#2006. Zirkzee refuses to blame referee after Manchester United’s Sporting draw

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Joshua Zirkzee believes Manchester United should take responsibility for their failure to beat Sporting on Wednesday rather than blame referee Szymon Marciniak.

Mauricio Pochettino’s side had two goals disallowed during the 2-2 Champions League draw in Lisbon. Bruno Fernandes scored twice against his former club early in the second half to put United ahead. However, an error by goalkeeper Senne Lammens allowed Luis Suarez to equalise with 10 minutes remaining.

“We shouldn’t look at the referee, we should look at ourselves instead,” Zirkzee told MUTV. Although frustrated by some of Marciniak’s decisions, the Dutchman felt United had sufficient quality to win the game regardless.

Wednesday’s appearance was Zirkzee’s first start for United since May’s goalless draw at Sunderland. The forward benefited from Matheus Cunha’s European suspension and received considerable vocal support from the travelling fans in Lisbon.

Zirkzee was replaced after 64 minutes, with Bryan Mbeumo moving into the centre-forward position. With Cunha available again for Saturday’s visit of Brentford, Pochettino has a decision to make over the composition of his attack.

United’s improvement in Lisbon was evident, particularly during the opening stages of the second half. However, their inability to protect a winning position ultimately proved more costly than any decision made by Marciniak.

Pochettino is still awaiting his first victory as United manager, something Zirkzee is clearly anxious to help deliver against Brentford.
